[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conspiracy relating to professional sport

- **[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  one who conspires, combines, agrees or arranges with another person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to limit unreasonably the opportunities for any
    other person to participate, as a player or competitor, in
    professional sport or to impose unreasonable terms or conditions on
    those persons who so participate, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to limit unreasonably the opportunity for any other
    person to negotiate with and, if agreement is reached, to play for
    the team or club of his choice in a professional league

  is guilty of an indictable offence and liable on conviction to a fine
  in the discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not
  exceeding five years or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Matters to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether or not an agreement or
  arrangement contravenes subsection (1), the court before which the
  contravention is alleged shall have regard to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether the sport in relation to which the
    contravention is alleged is organized on an international basis and,
    if so, whether any limitations, terms or conditions alleged should,
    for that reason, be accepted in Canada; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the desirability of maintaining a reasonable
    balance among the teams or clubs participating in the same league.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} This section applies, and section 45 does not apply,
  to agreements and arrangements and to provisions of agreements and
  arrangements between or among teams and clubs engaged in professional
  sport as members of the same league and between or among directors,
  officers or employees of those teams and clubs where the agreements,
  arrangements and provisions relate exclusively to matters described in
  subsection (1) or to the granting and operation of franchises in the
  league, and section 45 applies and this section does not apply to all
  other agreements, arrangements and provisions thereof between or among
  those teams, clubs and persons.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 76, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Agreements or arrangements of federal
financial institutions

- **[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), every
  federal financial institution that makes an agreement or arrangement
  with another federal financial institution with respect to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the rate of interest on a deposit,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the rate of interest or the charges on a loan,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amount or kind of any charge for a service
    provided to a customer,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the amount or kind of a loan to a customer,

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the kind of service to be provided to a customer,
    or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the person or classes of persons to whom a loan or
    other service will be made or provided or from whom a loan or other
    service will be withheld,

  and every director, officer or employee of the federal financial
  institution who knowingly makes such an agreement or arrangement on
  behalf of the federal financial institution is guilty of an indictable
  offence and liable to a fine not exceeding ten million dollars or to
  imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply in respect of an
  agreement or arrangement

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a deposit or loan made or payable
    outside Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} applicable only in respect of the dealings of or
    the services rendered between federal financial institutions or by
    two or more federal financial institutions as regards a customer of
    each of those federal financial institutions where the customer has
    knowledge of the agreement or by a federal financial institution as
    regards a customer thereof, on behalf of that customer's customers;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} with respect to a bid for or purchase, sale or
    underwriting of securities by federal financial institutions or a
    group including federal financial institutions;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} with respect to the exchange of statistics and
    credit information, the development and utilization of systems,
    forms, methods, procedures and standards, the utilization of common
    facilities and joint research and development in connection
    therewith, and the restriction of advertising;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} with respect to reasonable terms and conditions of
    participation in guaranteed or insured loan programs authorized
    pursuant to an Act of Parliament or of the legislature of a
    province;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} with respect to the amount of any charge for a
    service or with respect to the kind of service provided to a
    customer outside Canada, payable or performed outside Canada, or
    payable or performed in Canada on behalf of a person who is outside
    Canada;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} with respect to the persons or classes of persons
    to whom a loan or other service will be made or provided outside
    Canada;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in respect of which the Minister of Finance has
    certified to the Commissioner that Minister's request for or
    approval of the agreement or arrangement for the purposes of
    financial policy and has certified the names of the parties to the
    agreement or arrangement; or

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that is entered into only by financial institutions
    each of which is an affiliate of each of the others.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[federal financial
  institution]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section and section 45, [[federal financial
  institution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a bank or an authorized
  foreign bank within the meaning of section 2 of the [Bank
  Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), a company to which the [Trust and Loan
  Companies Act](/eng/acts/T-19.8) applies or a company or society to
  which the [Insurance Companies Act](/eng/acts/I-11.8) applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where proceedings commenced under
  section 76, 79, 90.1 or 92

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings may be commenced under this section
  against a person on the basis of facts that are the same or
  substantially the same as the facts on the basis of which an order
  against that person is sought by the Commissioner under section 76,
  79, 90.1 or 92.

::: HistoricalNote

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False or misleading representations

- **[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall, for the purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly,
  the supply or use of a product or for the purpose of promoting,
  directly or indirectly, any business interest, by any means whatever,
  knowingly or recklessly make a representation to the public that is
  false or misleading in a material respect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of certain matters not required

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, in establishing that
  subsection (1) was contravened, it is not necessary to prove that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person was deceived or misled;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any member of the public to whom the representation
    was made was within Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the representation was made in a place to which the
    public had access.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permitted representations

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, in this section and in
  sections 52.01, 52.1, 74.01, 74.011 and 74.02, the making or sending
  of a representation includes permitting a representation to be made or
  sent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drip pricing

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the making of a
  representation of a price that is not attainable due to fixed
  obligatory charges or fees constitutes a false or misleading
  representation, unless the obligatory charges or fees represent only
  an amount imposed on a purchaser of the product referred to in
  subsection (1) by or under an Act of Parliament or the legislature of
  a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations accompanying products

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a representation
  that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} expressed on an article offered or displayed for
    sale or its wrapper or container,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} expressed on anything attached to, inserted in or
    accompanying an article offered or displayed for sale, its wrapper
    or container, or anything on which the article is mounted for
    display or sale,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} expressed on an in-store or other point-of-purchase
    display,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} made in the course of in-store or door-to-door
    selling to a person as ultimate user, or by communicating orally by
    any means of telecommunication to a person as ultimate user, or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} contained in or on anything that is sold, sent,
    delivered, transmitted or made available in any other manner to a
    member of the public,

  is deemed to be made to the public by and only by the person who
  causes the representation to be so expressed, made or contained,
  subject to subsection (2.1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations from outside Canada

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Where a person referred to in subsection (2) is
  outside Canada, a representation described in paragraph (2)(a),
  (b), (c) or (e) is, for the purposes of subsection (1), deemed to be
  made to the public by the person who imports into Canada the article,
  thing or display referred to in that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed representation to public

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), a person who, for the
  purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, the supply or use of a
  product or any business interest, supplies to a wholesaler, retailer
  or other distributor of a product any material or thing that contains
  a representation of a nature referred to in subsection (1) is deemed
  to have made that representation to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General impression to be considered

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In a prosecution for a contravention of this section,
  the general impression conveyed by a representation as well as its
  literal meaning shall be taken into account in determining whether or
  not the representation is false or misleading in a material respect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any person who contravenes subsection (1) is guilty
  of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    14 years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$200,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or
    to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reviewable conduct

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in Part VII.1 shall be read as excluding the
  application of this section to a representation that constitutes
  reviewable conduct within the meaning of that Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duplication of proceedings

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings may be commenced under this section
  against a person against whom an order is, on application by the
  Commissioner, sought under Part VII.1 on the basis of the same or
  substantially the same facts as would be alleged in proceedings under
  this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 52
- 1999, c. 2, s. 12
- 2009, c. 2, s. 414
- 2010, c. 23, s. 74
- 2014, c. 31, s. 33

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False or misleading representation ---
sender or subject matter information

- **[[52.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No person shall, for the purpose of
  promoting, directly or indirectly, any business interest or the supply
  or use of a product, knowingly or recklessly send or cause to be sent
  a false or misleading representation in the sender information or
  subject matter information of an electronic message.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False or misleading representation ---
  electronic message

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall, for the purpose of promoting,
  directly or indirectly, any business interest or the supply or use of
  a product, knowingly or recklessly send or cause to be sent in an
  electronic message a representation that is false or misleading in a
  material respect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False or misleading representation ---
  locator

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall, for the purpose of promoting,
  directly or indirectly, any business interest or the supply or use of
  a product, knowingly or recklessly make or cause to be made a false or
  misleading representation in a locator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of deception not required

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, in establishing that any of
  subsections (1) to (3) was contravened, it is not necessary to prove
  that any person was deceived or misled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drip pricing

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the making of a
  representation of a price that is not attainable due to fixed
  obligatory charges or fees constitutes a false or misleading
  representation, unless the obligatory charges or fees represent only
  an amount imposed on a purchaser of the product referred to in
  subsections (1) to (3) by or under an Act of Parliament or the
  legislature of a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General impression to be considered

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In a prosecution for a contravention of any of
  subsections (1) to (3), the general impression conveyed by a
  representation as well as its literal meaning are to be taken into
  account.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any person who contravenes any of subsections (1)
  to (3) is guilty of an offence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} liable on conviction on indictment to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    14 years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} liable on summary conviction to a fine not
    exceeding \$200,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one
    year, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reviewable conduct

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in Part VII.1 is to be read as excluding the
  application of this section to the making of a representation that
  constitutes reviewable conduct within the meaning of that Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application made under Part VII.1

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} No proceedings may be commenced under this section
  against a person on the basis of facts that are the same or
  substantially the same as the facts on the basis of which an order
  against that person is, on application by the Commissioner, sought
  under Part VII.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an electronic message is considered to have been
    sent once its transmission has been initiated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is immaterial whether the electronic address to
    which an electronic message is sent exists or whether an electronic
    message reaches its intended destination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 23, s. 75
- [2024, c. 15, s. 234](#1462797-1484032){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462797-1484032.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assisting foreign states

- **[[52.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.02
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may, for the
  purpose of assisting an investigation or proceeding in respect of the
  laws of a foreign state, an international organization of states or an
  international organization established by the governments of states
  that address conduct that is substantially similar to conduct
  prohibited under section 52, 52.01, 52.1, 53, 55 or 55.1,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct any investigation that the Commissioner
    considers necessary to collect relevant information, using any
    powers that the Commissioner may use under this Act or the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46) to investigate an offence under any of those
    sections; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disclose the information to the government of the
    foreign state or to the international organization, or to any
    institution of any such government or organization responsible for
    conducting investigations or initiating proceedings in respect of
    the laws in respect of which the assistance is being provided, if
    the government, organization or institution declares in writing that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the use of the information will be restricted to
      purposes relevant to the investigation or proceeding, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the information will be treated in a
      confidential manner and, except for the purposes mentioned in
      subparagraph (i), will not be further disclosed without the
      Commissioner's express consent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mutual assistance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to provide assistance under
  subsection (1), the Commissioner shall consider whether the
  government, organization or institution agrees to provide assistance
  for investigations or proceedings in respect of any of the sections
  mentioned in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 23, s. 75
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[telemarketing]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[52.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[telemarketing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the practice
  of communicating orally by any means of telecommunication for the
  purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, any business interest or
  the supply or use of a product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Required disclosures

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall engage in telemarketing unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} disclosure is made, in a fair and reasonable manner
    at the beginning of each communication, of the identity of the
    person on behalf of whom the communication is made, the nature of
    the business interest or product being promoted and the purposes of
    the communication;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disclosure is made, in a fair, reasonable and
    timely manner, of the price of any product whose supply or use is
    being promoted and any material restrictions, terms or conditions
    applicable to its delivery; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} disclosure is made, in a fair, reasonable and
    timely manner, of such other information in relation to the product
    as may be prescribed by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deceptive telemarketing

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person who engages in telemarketing shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} make a representation that is false or misleading
    in a material respect;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct or purport to conduct a contest, lottery or
    game of chance, skill or mixed chance and skill, where

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the delivery of a prize or other benefit to a
      participant in the contest, lottery or game is, or is represented
      to be, conditional on the prior payment of any amount by the
      participant, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} adequate and fair disclosure is not made of the
      number and approximate value of the prizes, of the area or areas
      to which they relate and of any fact within the person's
      knowledge, that affects materially the chances of winning;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} offer a product at no cost, or at a price less than
    the fair market value of the product, in consideration of the supply
    or use of another product, unless fair, reasonable and timely
    disclosure is made of the fair market value of the first product and
    of any restrictions, terms or conditions applicable to its supply to
    the purchaser; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} offer a product for sale at a price grossly in
    excess of its fair market value, where delivery of the product is,
    or is represented to be, conditional on prior payment by the
    purchaser.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General impression to be considered

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In a prosecution for a contravention of paragraph
  (3)(a), the general impression conveyed by a representation as well as
  its literal meaning shall be taken into account in determining whether
  or not the representation is false or misleading in a material
  respect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time of disclosure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The disclosure of information referred to in
  paragraph (2)(b) or (c) or (3)(b) or (c) must be made during the
  course of a communication unless it is established by the accused that
  the information was disclosed within a reasonable time before the
  communication, by any means, and the information was not requested
  during the communication.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be convicted of an offence under this
  section who establishes that the person exercised due diligence to
  prevent the commission of the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences by employees or agents

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding subsection (6), in the prosecution of
  a corporation for an offence under this section, it is sufficient
  proof of the offence to establish that it was committed by an employee
  or agent of the corporation, whether or not the employee or agent is
  identified, unless the corporation establishes that the corporation
  exercised due diligence to prevent the commission of the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of officers and directors

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Where a corporation commits an offence under this
  section, any officer or director of the corporation who is in a
  position to direct or influence the policies of the corporation in
  respect of conduct prohibited by this section is a party to and guilty
  of the offence and is liable to the punishment provided for the
  offence, whether or not the corporation has been prosecuted or
  convicted, unless the officer or director establishes that the officer
  or director exercised due diligence to prevent the commission of the
  offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} Any person who contravenes subsection (2) or (3) is
  guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    14 years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$200,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or
    to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentencing

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} In sentencing a person convicted of an offence under
  this section, the court shall consider, among other factors, the
  following aggravating factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the use of lists of persons previously deceived by
    means of telemarketing;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} characteristics of the persons to whom the
    telemarketing was directed, including classes of persons who are
    especially vulnerable to abusive tactics;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the proceeds realized by the person
    from the telemarketing;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} previous convictions of the person under this
    section or under section 52 in respect of conduct prohibited by this
    section; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which information is conveyed,
    including the use of abusive tactics.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 13
- 2009, c. 2, s. 415
- 2010, c. 23, s. 76
- 2014, c. 31, s. 34
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-52.1-20140701.html "Link to previous version of section 52.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deceptive notice of winning a prize

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall, for the purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly,
  any business interest or the supply or use of a product, send or cause
  to be sent by electronic or regular mail or by any other means a
  document or notice in any form, if the document or notice gives the
  general impression that the recipient has won, will win, or will on
  doing a particular act win, a prize or other benefit, and if the
  recipient is asked or given the option to pay money, incur a cost or
  do anything that will incur a cost.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the recipient
  actually wins the prize or other benefit and the person who sends or
  causes the notice or document to be sent

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes adequate and fair disclosure of the number
    and approximate value of the prizes or benefits, of the area or
    areas to which they have been allocated and of any fact within the
    person's knowledge that materially affects the chances of winning;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} distributes the prizes or benefits without
    unreasonable delay; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} selects participants or distributes the prizes or
    benefits randomly, or on the basis of the participants' skill, in
    any area to which the prizes or benefits have been allocated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be convicted of an offence under this
  section who establishes that the person exercised due diligence to
  prevent the commission of the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offences by employees or agents

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In the prosecution of a corporation for an offence
  under this section, it is sufficient proof of the offence to establish
  that it was committed by an employee or agent of the corporation,
  whether or not the employee or agent is identified, unless the
  corporation establishes that the corporation exercised due diligence
  to prevent the commission of the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of officers and directors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a corporation commits an offence under this
  section, any officer or director of the corporation who is in a
  position to direct or influence the policies of the corporation in
  respect of conduct prohibited by this section is a party to and guilty
  of the offence and is liable to the punishment provided for the
  offence, whether or not the corporation has been prosecuted or
  convicted, unless the officer or director establishes that the officer
  or director exercised due diligence to prevent the commission of the
  offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any person who contravenes this section is guilty of
  an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    14 years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$200,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or
    to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sentencing

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In sentencing a person convicted of an offence under
  this section, the court shall consider, among other factors, the
  following aggravating factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the use of lists of persons previously deceived by
    the commission of an offence under section 52.1 or this section;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the particular vulnerability of recipients of the
    notices or documents referred to in subsection (1) to abusive
    tactics;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the amount of the proceeds realized by the person
    from the commission of an offence under this section;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} previous convictions of the person under section 52
    or 52.1 or this section; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which information is conveyed,
    including the use of abusive tactics.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 53
- 1999, c. 2, s. 14
- 2002, c. 16, s. 6
- 2009, c. 2, s. 416
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-53-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 53"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Double ticketing

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall supply a product at a price that exceeds the lowest of
  two or more prices clearly expressed by him or on his behalf, in
  respect of the product in the quantity in which it is so supplied and
  at the time at which it is so supplied,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the product, its wrapper or container;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on anything attached to, inserted in or
    accompanying the product, its wrapper or container or anything on
    which the product is mounted for display or sale; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} on an in-store or other point-of-purchase display
    or advertisement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person who contravenes subsection (1) is guilty
  of an offence and liable on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding
  ten thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one
  year or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1974-75-76, c. 76, s. 18
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[multi-level marketing
plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section and section 55.1, [[multi-level marketing
  plan]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a plan for the supply of a product
  whereby a participant in the plan receives compensation for the supply
  of the product to another participant in the plan who, in turn,
  receives compensation for the supply of the same or another product to
  other participants in the plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations as to compensation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person who operates or participates in a
  multi-level marketing plan shall make any representations relating to
  compensation under the plan to a prospective participant in the plan
  unless the representations constitute or include fair, reasonable and
  timely disclosure of the information within the knowledge of the
  person making the representations relating to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} compensation actually received by typical
    participants in the plan; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} compensation likely to be received by typical
    participants in the plan, having regard to any relevant
    considerations, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the product, including its price
      and availability,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the relevant market for the
      product,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the nature of the plan and similar plans, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} whether the person who operates the plan is a
      corporation, partnership, sole proprietorship or other form of
      business organization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A person who operates a multi-level marketing plan
  shall ensure that any representations relating to compensation under
  the plan that are made to a prospective participant in the plan by a
  participant in the plan or by a representative of the person who
  operates the plan constitute or include fair, reasonable and timely
  disclosure of the information within the knowledge of the person who
  operates the plan relating to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} compensation actually received by typical
    participants in the plan; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} compensation likely to be received by typical
    participants in the plan, having regard to any relevant
    considerations, including those specified in paragraph (2)(b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Due diligence defence

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} A person accused of an offence under subsection
  (2.1) shall not be convicted of the offence if the accused establishes
  that he or she took reasonable precautions and exercised due diligence
  to ensure

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that no representations relating to compensation
    under the plan were made by participants in the plan or by
    representatives of the accused; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that any representations relating to compensation
    under the plan that were made by participants in the plan or by
    representatives of the accused constituted or included fair,
    reasonable and timely disclosure of the information referred to in
    that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any person who contravenes subsection (2) or (2.1) is
  guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    five years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$200,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or
    to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 55
- 1992, c. 14, s. 1
- 1999, c. 2, s. 15
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[scheme of pyramid
selling]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[55.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
  [[scheme of pyramid selling]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a multi-level
  marketing plan whereby

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a participant in the plan gives consideration for
    the right to receive compensation by reason of the recruitment into
    the plan of another participant in the plan who gives consideration
    for the same right;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a participant in the plan gives consideration, as a
    condition of participating in the plan, for a specified amount of
    the product, other than a specified amount of the product that is
    bought at the seller's cost price for the purpose only of
    facilitating sales;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person knowingly supplies the product to a
    participant in the plan in an amount that is commercially
    unreasonable; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a participant in the plan who is supplied with the
    product

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} does not have a buy-back guarantee that is
      exercisable on reasonable commercial terms or a right to return
      the product in saleable condition on reasonable commercial terms,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is not informed of the existence of the
      guarantee or right and the manner in which it can be exercised.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Pyramid selling

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall establish, operate, advertise or
  promote a scheme of pyramid selling.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any person who contravenes subsection (2) is guilty
  of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    five years or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
    \$200,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or
    to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1992, c. 14, s. 1
- 1999, c. 2, s. 16
:::

**[[56 to 59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56_to_59 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
1999, c. 2, s. 17\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defence

**[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** Section 54 does not apply
to a person who prints or publishes or otherwise distributes a
representation or an advertisement on behalf of another person in Canada
if he or she establishes that he or she obtained and recorded the name
and address of that other person and accepted the representation or
advertisement in good faith for printing, publishing or other
distribution in the ordinary course of his or her business.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 60
- 1999, c. 2, s. 17.1
:::

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2009, c. 2,
s. 417\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil rights not affected

**[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided in this Part, nothing in this Part shall be construed as
depriving any person of any civil right of action.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., c. C-23, s. 39
- 1974-75-76, c. 76, s. 18
:::

## [PART VII]{.HLabel1}[Other Offences]{.HTitleText1} {#h-89016 .Part}

### [Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89017 .Subheading}

**[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, R.S., 1985,
c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 37\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obstruction

- **[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall in any manner impede or prevent or attempt to impede or
  prevent any inquiry or examination under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence and punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1) is guilty
  of an offence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} liable on conviction on indictment to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    10 years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} liable on summary conviction to a fine not
    exceeding \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two
    years, or to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 64
- 2009, c. 2, s. 418
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-64-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 64"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of Part II provisions

- **[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person who, without good and sufficient cause, the proof of which lies
  on that person, fails to comply with an order made under section 11
  and every person who contravenes subsection 15(5) or 16(2) is guilty
  of an offence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} liable on conviction on indictment to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    two years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} liable on summary conviction to a fine not
    exceeding \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two
    years, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to supply information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, without good and sufficient cause,
  the proof of which lies on that person, contravenes subsection 114(1)
  is guilty of an offence and liable on conviction on indictment or on
  summary conviction to a fine not exceeding \$50,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction or alteration of records or
  things

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who destroys or alters, or causes to be
  destroyed or altered, any record or other thing that is required to be
  produced under section 11 or in respect of which a warrant is issued
  under section 15 is guilty of an offence and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} liable on conviction on indictment to a fine in the
    discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
    10 years, or to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} liable on summary conviction to a fine not
    exceeding \$100,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two
    years, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of directors

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a corporation commits an offence under this
  section, any officer, director or agent of the corporation who
  directed, authorized, assented to, acquiesced in or participated in
  the commission of the offence is a party to and guilty of the offence
  and is liable to the punishment provided for the offence whether or
  not the corporation has been prosecuted or convicted.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 65
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 38
- 1999, c. 2, s. 18
- 2009, c. 2, s. 419
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-65-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 65"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of subsection 30.06(5)

- **[[65.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, without good and
  sufficient cause, the proof of which lies on that person, contravenes
  subsection 30.06(5) is guilty of an offence and liable on summary
  conviction or on conviction on indictment to a fine not exceeding
  \$5,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two years, or to
  both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction or alteration of records or
  things

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who destroys or alters, or causes to be
  destroyed or altered, any record or thing in respect of which a search
  warrant is issued under section 30.06 or that is required to be
  produced pursuant to an order made under subsection 30.11(1) or
  30.16(1) is guilty of an offence and liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment to a fine not exceeding
    \$50,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years, or
    to both; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction to a fine not exceeding
    \$25,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two years, or
    to both.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 16, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal after objection overruled

- **[[65.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every person who, without good and
  sufficient cause, the proof of which lies on that person, refuses to
  answer a question or to produce a record or thing to the person
  designated under paragraph 30.11(2)(c) after a judge has ruled against
  the objection under paragraph 30.11(8)(a), is guilty of an offence and
  liable on conviction on indictment or on summary conviction to a fine
  not exceeding \$5,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two
  years, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal where no ruling made on
  objection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person is guilty of an offence and liable on
  summary conviction or on conviction on indictment to a fine not
  exceeding \$5,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding two
  years, or to both, who, without good and sufficient cause, the proof
  of which lies on that person, refuses to answer a question or to
  produce a record or thing to the person designated under paragraph
  30.11(2)(c), where no ruling has been made under paragraph
  30.11(8)(a),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} without giving the detailed statement required by
    subsection 30.11(9); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the person was previously asked the same
    question or requested to produce the same record or thing and
    refused to do so and the reasons on which that person based the
    previous refusal were determined not to be well-founded by

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a judge, if the reasons were based on the
      Canadian law of non-disclosure of information or privilege, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a court of the foreign state or by a person
      designated by the foreign state, if the reasons were based on a
      law that applies to the foreign state.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 16, s. 7
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of order under Part VII.1
or VIII

**[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** Every person who
contravenes an order made under Part VII.1, except paragraphs 74.1(1)(c)
and (d), or under Part VIII, except subsection 79(3.1), is guilty of an
offence and liable

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} on conviction on indictment, to a fine in the
  discretion of the court or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
  five years, or to both; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} on summary conviction, to a fine not exceeding
  \$25,000 or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one year, or to
  both.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 66
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 39
- 1999, c. 2, s. 19
- 2009, c. 2, s. 420
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-66-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 66"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Whistleblowing

- **[[66.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any person who has reasonable
  grounds to believe that a person has committed or intends to commit an
  offence under the Act, may notify the Commissioner of the particulars
  of the matter and may request that his or her identity be kept
  confidential with respect to the notification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confidentiality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall keep confidential the identity
  of a person who has notified the Commissioner under subsection (1) and
  to whom an assurance of confidentiality has been provided by any
  person who performs duties or functions in the administration or
  enforcement of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 19
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[66.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  employer shall dismiss, suspend, demote, discipline, harass or
  otherwise disadvantage an employee, or deny an employee a benefit of
  employment, by reason that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has disclosed to the Commissioner that the
    employer or any other person has committed or intends to commit an
    offence under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has refused or stated an intention of refusing
    to do anything that is an offence under this Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has done or stated an intention of doing
    anything that is required to be done in order that an offence not be
    committed under this Act; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the employer believes that the employee will do
    anything referred to in paragraph (a) or (c) or will refuse to do
    anything referred to in paragraph (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section impairs any right of an
  employee either at law or under an employment contract or collective
  agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this section, [[employee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  includes an independent contractor and
  [[employer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the corresponding meaning.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 19
:::

### [Procedure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89089 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure for enforcing punishment

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  an indictment is found against an accused, other than a corporation,
  for any offence against this Act, the accused may elect to be tried
  without a jury and where he so elects, he shall be tried by the judge
  presiding at the court at which the indictment is found, or the judge
  presiding at any subsequent sittings of that court, or at any court
  where the indictment comes on for trial.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where an election is made under subsection (1), the
  proceedings subsequent to the election shall be regulated in so far as
  may be applicable by the provisions of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) relating to the trial of indictable offences by
  a judge without a jury.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of courts

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No court other than a superior court of criminal
  jurisdiction, as defined in the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), has
  power to try any offence under section 45, 46, 47, 48 or 49.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Corporations --- trials with or without
  jury

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite anything in the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or in any other statute or law, the following
  rules apply to corporations charged with an offence under this Act:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if one or more corporations are charged and no
    individual is charged in the same indictment, the corporation or
    corporations are to be tried without a jury;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if one or more corporations and a single individual
    are charged in the same indictment, then, unless the court is
    satisfied that the ends of justice require otherwise, the
    corporation or corporations are to be tried

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} without a jury if the individual elects or
      re-elects to be tried without a jury, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with a jury if the individual elects or
      re-elects to be tried with a jury; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if one or more corporations and two or more
    individuals are charged in the same indictment, then, unless the
    court is satisfied that the ends of justice require otherwise, the
    corporation or corporations are to be tried

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} without a jury if all the individuals elect or
      re-elect to be tried without a jury,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} with a jury if all the individuals elect or
      re-elect to be tried with a jury, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} either with or without a jury, as determined by
      the Attorney General of Canada for each corporation, if some but
      not all of the individuals elect or re-elect to be tried without a
      jury.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Option as to procedure under subsection
  34(2)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In any case where subsection 34(2) is applicable, the
  Attorney General of Canada or the attorney general of the province may
  in his discretion institute proceedings either by way of an
  information under that subsection or by way of prosecution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings in respect of an offence that is declared
  by this Act to be punishable on summary conviction may be instituted
  at any time within but not later than two years after the time when
  the subject-matter of the proceedings arose.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 67
- [2024, c. 15, s. 235](#1462802-1484034){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462802-1484034.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-67-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 67"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Venue of prosecutions

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding any other
Act, a prosecution for an offence under Part VI or section 66 may be
brought, in addition to any place in which the prosecution may be
brought by virtue of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} where the accused is a corporation, in any
  territorial division in which the corporation has its head office or a
  branch office, whether or not the branch office is provided for in any
  Act or instrument relating to the incorporation or organization of the
  corporation; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} where the accused is not a corporation, in any
  territorial division in which the accused resides or has a place of
  business.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 68
- 1999, c. 2, s. 20
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section,

  [[agent of a participant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[agent of a participant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who
      by a record admitted in evidence under this section appears to be
      or is otherwise proven to be an officer, agent, servant, employee
      or representative of a participant; ([agent d'un
      participant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}[\[Repealed, R.S., 1985, c. 19
      (2nd Supp.), s. 40\]]{.Repealed}

  [[participant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[participant]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any person against whom
      proceedings have been instituted under this Act and in the case of
      a prosecution means any accused and any person who, although not
      accused, is alleged in the charge or indictment to have been a
      co-conspirator or otherwise party or privy to the offence
      charged. ([participant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence against a participant

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In any proceedings before the Tribunal or in any
  prosecution or proceedings before a court under or pursuant to this
  Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} anything done, said or agreed on by an agent of a
    participant shall, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, be
    deemed to have been done, said or agreed on, as the case may be,
    with the authority of that participant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a record written or received by an agent of a
    participant shall, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, be
    deemed to have been written or received, as the case may be, with
    the authority of that participant; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a record proved to have been in the possession of a
    participant or on premises used or occupied by a participant or in
    the possession of an agent of a participant shall be admitted in
    evidence without further proof thereof and is [prima
    facie]{.otherLang lang="la"} proof

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that the participant had knowledge of the record
      and its contents,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} that anything recorded in or by the record as
      having been done, said or agreed on by any participant or by an
      agent of a participant was done, said or agreed on as recorded
      and, where anything is recorded in or by the record as having been
      done, said or agreed on by an agent of a participant, that it was
      done, said or agreed on with the authority of that participant,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} that the record, where it appears to have been
      written by any participant or by an agent of a participant, was so
      written and, where it appears to have been written by an agent of
      a participant, that it was written with the authority of that
      participant.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 69
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 40
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Admissibility of statistics

- **[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  collection, compilation, analysis, abstract or other record or report
  of statistical information prepared or published under the authority
  of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the [Statistics Act](/eng/acts/S-19), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any other enactment of Parliament or of the
    legislature of a province,

  is admissible in evidence in any proceedings before the Tribunal or in
  any prosecution or proceedings before a court under or pursuant to
  this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On request from the Minister or the Commissioner

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Chief Statistician of Canada or an officer of
    any department or agency of the Government of Canada the functions
    of which include the gathering of statistics shall, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an officer of any department or agency of the
    government of a province the functions of which include the
    gathering of statistics may,

  compile from his or its records a statement of statistics relating to
  any industry or sector thereof, in accordance with the terms of the
  request, and any such statement is admissible in evidence in any
  proceedings before the Tribunal or in any prosecution or proceedings
  before a court under or pursuant to this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Privileged information not affected

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section compels or authorizes the
  Chief Statistician of Canada or any officer of a department or agency
  of the Government of Canada to disclose any particulars relating to an
  individual or business in a manner that is prohibited by any provision
  of an enactment of Parliament or of a provincial legislature designed
  for the protection of those particulars.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In any proceedings before the Tribunal, or in any
  prosecution or proceedings before a court under or pursuant to this
  Act, a certificate purporting to be signed by the Chief Statistician
  of Canada or the officer of the department or agency of the Government
  of Canada or of a province under whose supervision a record, report or
  statement of statistics referred to in this section was prepared,
  setting out that the record, report or statement of statistics
  attached thereto was prepared under his supervision, is evidence of
  the facts alleged therein without proof of the signature or official
  character of the person by whom it purports to be signed.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 70
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 41
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statistics collected by sampling methods

**[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** A collection,
compilation, analysis, abstract or other record or report of statistics
collected by sampling methods by or on behalf of the Commissioner or any
other party to proceedings before the Tribunal, or to a prosecution or
proceedings before a court under or pursuant to this Act, is admissible
in evidence in that prosecution or those proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 71
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 42
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

- **[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  record, report or statement of statistical information or statistics
  referred to in section 70 or 71 shall be received in evidence before
  the Tribunal or court unless the person intending to produce the
  record, report or statement in evidence has given to the person
  against whom it is intended to be produced reasonable notice together
  with a copy of the record, report or statement and, in the case of a
  record or report of statistics referred to in section 71, together
  with the names and qualifications of those persons who participated in
  the preparation thereof.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attendance of statistician

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any person against whom a record or report of
  statistics referred to in section 70 is produced may require, for the
  purposes of cross-examination, the attendance of any person under
  whose supervision the record or report was prepared.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Idem

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any person against whom a record or report of
  statistics referred to in section 71 is produced may require, for the
  purposes of cross-examination, the attendance of any person who
  participated in the preparation of the record or report.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 72
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 43
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of Federal Court

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this section, the
  Attorney General of Canada may institute and conduct any prosecution
  or other proceedings under section 34, any of sections 45 to 49 or, if
  the proceedings are on indictment, under section 52, 52.1, 53, 55,
  55.1 or 66, in the Federal Court, and for the purposes of the
  prosecution or other proceedings, the Federal Court has all the powers
  and jurisdiction of a superior court of criminal jurisdiction under
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) and under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No jury

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The trial of an offence under Part VI or section 66
  in the Federal Court shall be without a jury.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An appeal lies from the Federal Court to the Federal
  Court of Appeal and from the Federal Court of Appeal to the Supreme
  Court of Canada in any prosecution or proceedings under Part VI or
  section 66 of this Act as provided in Part XXI of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) for appeals from a trial court and from a court
  of appeal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proceedings optional

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Proceedings under subsection 34(2) may in the
  discretion of the Attorney General of Canada be instituted in either
  the Federal Court or a superior court of criminal jurisdiction in the
  province but no prosecution shall be instituted against an individual
  in the Federal Court in respect of an offence under Part VI or section
  66 without the consent of the individual.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 73
- 1999, c. 2, s. 21
- 2002, c. 8, ss. 183, 198, c. 16, s. 8
- 2009, c. 2, s. 421
:::
