Version](section-73-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 73"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 1999, c. 2,
s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

## [PART VII.1]{.HLabel1}[Deceptive Marketing Practices]{.HTitleText1} {#h-89169 .Part}

### [Reviewable Matters]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89170 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Misrepresentations to public

- **[[74.01]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.01
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person engages in reviewable
  conduct who, for the purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, the
  supply or use of a product or for the purpose of promoting, directly
  or indirectly, any business interest, by any means whatever,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} makes a representation to the public that is false
    or misleading in a material respect;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} makes a representation to the public in the form of
    a statement, warranty or guarantee of the performance, efficacy or
    length of life of a product that is not based on an adequate and
    proper test thereof, the proof of which lies on the person making
    the representation;

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} makes a representation to the public in the form
    of a statement, warranty or guarantee of a product's benefits for
    protecting or restoring the environment or mitigating the
    environmental, social and ecological causes or effects of climate
    change that is not based on an adequate and proper test, the proof
    of which lies on the person making the representation;

  - [(b.2)]{.lawlabel} makes a representation to the public with respect
    to the benefits of a business or business activity for protecting or
    restoring the environment or mitigating the environmental and
    ecological causes or effects of climate change that is not based on
    adequate and proper substantiation in accordance with
    internationally recognized methodology, the proof of which lies on
    the person making the representation; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} makes a representation to the public in a form that
    purports to be

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a warranty or guarantee of a product, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a promise to replace, maintain or repair an
      article or any part thereof or to repeat or continue a service
      until it has achieved a specified result,

    if the form of purported warranty or guarantee or promise is
    materially misleading or if there is no reasonable prospect that it
    will be carried out.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drip pricing

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the making of a
  representation of a price that is not attainable due to fixed
  obligatory charges or fees constitutes a false or misleading
  representation, unless the obligatory charges or fees represent only
  an amount imposed on a purchaser of the product referred to in
  subsection (1) by or under an Act of Parliament or the legislature of
  a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ordinary price: suppliers generally

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), a person engages in
  reviewable conduct who, for the purpose of promoting, directly or
  indirectly, the supply or use of a product or for the purpose of
  promoting, directly or indirectly, any business interest, by any means
  whatever, makes a representation to the public concerning the price at
  which a product or like products have been, are or will be ordinarily
  supplied where suppliers generally in the relevant geographic market,
  having regard to the nature of the product,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have not sold a substantial volume of the product
    at that price or a higher price within a reasonable period of time
    before or after the making of the representation, as the case may
    be; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have not offered the product at that price or a
    higher price in good faith for a substantial period of time recently
    before or immediately after the making of the representation, as the
    case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ordinary price: supplier's own

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person engages in reviewable conduct who, for the
  purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, the supply or use of a
  product or for the purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, any
  business interest, by any means, makes a representation to the public
  as to the price at which a product or like products have been, are or
  will be ordinarily supplied by the person making the representation
  unless that person, having regard to the nature of the product and the
  relevant geographic market, establishes that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} they have sold a substantial volume of the product
    at that price or a higher price within a reasonable period of time
    before or after the making of the representation, as the case may
    be; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} they have offered the product at that price or a
    higher price in good faith for a substantial period of time recently
    before or immediately after the making of the representation, as the
    case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}References to time in subsections (2)
  and (3)

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, whether the period of time to
  be considered in paragraphs (2)(a) and (b) and (3)(a) and (b) is
  before or after the making of the representation depends on whether
  the representation relates to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the price at which products have been or are
    supplied; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the price at which products will be supplied.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (2) and (3) do not apply to a person who
  establishes that, in the circumstances, a representation as to price
  is not false or misleading in a material respect.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2009, c. 2, s. 422\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2009, c. 2, s. 422
- [2022, c. 10, s. 259](#1357334-1366440){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357334-1366440.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 236](#1462804-1484054){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462804-1484054.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74.01-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 74.01"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False or misleading representation ---
sender or subject matter information

- **[[74.011]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.011
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person engages in reviewable
  conduct who, for the purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, any
  business interest or the supply or use of a product, sends or causes
  to be sent a false or misleading representation in the sender
  information or subject matter information of an electronic message.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False or misleading representation ---
  electronic message

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person engages in reviewable conduct who, for the
  purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, any business interest or
  the supply or use of a product, sends or causes to be sent in an
  electronic message a representation that is false or misleading in a
  material respect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}False or misleading representation ---
  locator

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person engages in reviewable conduct who, for the
  purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, any business interest or
  the supply or use of a product, makes or causes to be made a false or
  misleading representation in a locator.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drip pricing

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the making of a
  representation of a price that is not attainable due to fixed
  obligatory charges or fees constitutes a false or misleading
  representation, unless the obligatory charges or fees represent only
  an amount imposed on a purchaser of the product referred to in
  subsections (1) to (3) by or under an Act of Parliament or the
  legislature of a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Proof of deception not required

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, in determining whether or
  not the person who made the representation engaged in the reviewable
  conduct, it is not necessary to establish that any person was deceived
  or misled.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General impression to be considered

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings under this section, the general
  impression conveyed by a representation as well as its literal meaning
  shall be taken into account in determining whether or not the person
  who made the representation engaged in the reviewable conduct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an electronic message is considered to have been
    sent once its transmission has been initiated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} it is immaterial whether the electronic address to
    which an electronic message is sent exists or whether an electronic
    message reaches its intended destination.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 23, s. 77
- [2024, c. 15, s. 237](#1462811-1484055){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462811-1484055.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74.011-20140701.html "Link to previous version of section 74.011"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assisting foreign states

- **[[74.012]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.012
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may, for the
  purpose of assisting an investigation or proceeding in respect of the
  laws of a foreign state, an international organization of states or an
  international organization established by the governments of states
  that address conduct that is substantially similar to conduct that is
  reviewable under section 74.01, 74.011, 74.02, 74.04, 74.05 or 74.06,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct any investigation that the Commissioner
    considers necessary to collect relevant information, using any
    powers that the Commissioner may use under this Act to investigate
    conduct that is reviewable under any of those sections; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} disclose the information to the government of the
    foreign state or to the international organization, or to any
    institution of any such government or organization responsible for
    conducting investigations or initiating proceedings in respect of
    the laws in respect of which the assistance is being provided, if
    the government, organization or institution declares in writing that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the use of the information will be restricted to
      purposes relevant to the investigation or proceeding, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the information will be treated in a
      confidential manner and, except for the purposes mentioned in
      subparagraph (i), will not be further disclosed without the
      Commissioner's express consent.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the contravention of
  the laws of the foreign state has consequences that would be
  considered penal under Canadian law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Mutual assistance

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to provide assistance under
  subsection (1), the Commissioner shall consider whether the
  government, organization or institution agrees to provide assistance
  for investigations or proceedings in respect of any of the sections
  mentioned in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 23, s. 77
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representation as to reasonable test and
publication of testimonials

**[[74.02]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.02 .sectionLabel}** A person engages in
reviewable conduct who, for the purpose of promoting, directly or
indirectly, the supply or use of any product, or for the purpose of
promoting, directly or indirectly, any business interest, makes a
representation to the public that a test has been made as to the
performance, efficacy or length of life of a product by any person, or
publishes a testimonial with respect to a product, unless the person
making the representation or publishing the testimonial can establish
that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} such a representation or testimonial was previously
  made or published by the person by whom the test was made or the
  testimonial was given, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} such a representation or testimonial was, before
  being made or published, approved and permission to make or publish it
  was given in writing by the person by whom the test was made or the
  testimonial was given,

and the representation or testimonial accords with the representation or
testimonial previously made, published or approved.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations accompanying products

- **[[74.03]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.03
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of sections 74.01
  and 74.02, a representation that is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} expressed on an article offered or displayed for
    sale or its wrapper or container,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} expressed on anything attached to, inserted in or
    accompanying an article offered or displayed for sale, its wrapper
    or container, or anything on which the article is mounted for
    display or sale,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} expressed on an in-store or other point-of-purchase
    display,

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} made in the course of in-store or door-to-door
    selling to a person as ultimate user, or by communicating orally by
    any means of telecommunication to a person as ultimate user, or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} contained in or on anything that is sold, sent,
    delivered, transmitted or made available in any other manner to a
    member of the public,

  is deemed to be made to the public by and only by the person who
  causes the representation to be so expressed, made or contained,
  subject to subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations from outside Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person referred to in subsection (1) is
  outside Canada, a representation described in paragraph (1)(a),
  (b), (c) or (e) is, for the purposes of sections 74.01 and 74.02,
  deemed to be made to the public by the person who imports into Canada
  the article, thing or display referred to in that paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed representation to public

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (1), a person who, for the
  purpose of promoting, directly or indirectly, the supply or use of a
  product or any business interest, supplies to a wholesaler, retailer
  or other distributor of a product any material or thing that contains
  a representation of a nature referred to in section 74.01 is deemed to
  make that representation to the public.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certain matters need not be established

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, in proceedings under sections
  74.01 and 74.02, it is not necessary to establish that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person was deceived or misled;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any member of the public to whom the representation
    was made was within Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the representation was made in a place to which the
    public had access.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General impression to be considered

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} In proceedings under sections 74.01 and 74.02, the
  general impression conveyed by a representation as well as its literal
  meaning shall be taken into account in determining whether or not the
  person who made the representation engaged in the reviewable conduct.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2009, c. 2, s. 423
- 2010, c. 23, s. 78
- 2014, c. 31, s. 35
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74.03-20140701.html "Link to previous version of section 74.03"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[bargain
price]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[74.04]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.04
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section,
  [[bargain price]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a price that is represented in an advertisement to
    be a bargain price by reference to an ordinary price or otherwise;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a price that a person who reads, hears or sees the
    advertisement would reasonably understand to be a bargain price by
    reason of the prices at which the product advertised or like
    products are ordinarily supplied.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Bait and switch selling

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person engages in reviewable conduct who advertises
  at a bargain price a product that the person does not supply in
  reasonable quantities having regard to the nature of the market in
  which the person carries on business, the nature and size of the
  person's business and the nature of the advertisement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (2) does not apply to a person who
  establishes that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person took reasonable steps to obtain in
    adequate time a quantity of the product that would have been
    reasonable having regard to the nature of the advertisement, but was
    unable to obtain such a quantity by reason of events beyond the
    person's control that could not reasonably have been anticipated;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person obtained a quantity of the product that
    was reasonable having regard to the nature of the advertisement, but
    was unable to meet the demand therefor because that demand surpassed
    the person's reasonable expectations; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} after becoming unable to supply the product in
    accordance with the advertisement, the person undertook to supply
    the same product or an equivalent product of equal or better quality
    at the bargain price and within a reasonable time to all persons who
    requested the product and who were not supplied with it during the
    time when the bargain price applied, and the person fulfilled the
    undertaking.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sale above advertised price

- **[[74.05]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.05
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A person engages in reviewable
  conduct who advertises a product for sale or rent in a market and,
  during the period and in the market to which the advertisement
  relates, supplies the product at a price that is higher than the price
  advertised.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an advertisement that appears in a
    catalogue in which it is prominently stated that the prices
    contained in it are subject to error if the person establishes that
    the price advertised is in error;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an advertisement that is immediately
    followed by another advertisement correcting the price mentioned in
    the first advertisement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the supply of a security obtained on
    the open market during a period when the prospectus relating to that
    security is still current; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in respect of the supply of a product by or on
    behalf of a person who is not engaged in the business of dealing in
    that product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, the market to which
  an advertisement relates is the market that the advertisement could
  reasonably be expected to reach, unless the advertisement defines the
  market more narrowly by reference to a geographical area, store,
  department of a store, sale by catalogue or otherwise.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Promotional contests

**[[74.06]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.06 .sectionLabel}** A person engages in
reviewable conduct who, for the purpose of promoting, directly or
indirectly, the supply or use of a product, or for the purpose of
promoting, directly or indirectly, any business interest, conducts any
contest, lottery, game of chance or skill, or mixed chance and skill, or
otherwise disposes of any product or other benefit by any mode of
chance, skill or mixed chance and skill whatever, where

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} adequate and fair disclosure is not made of the
  number and approximate value of the prizes, of the area or areas to
  which they relate and of any fact within the knowledge of the person
  that affects materially the chances of winning;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} distribution of the prizes is unduly delayed; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} selection of participants or distribution of prizes
  is not made on the basis of skill or on a random basis in any area to
  which prizes have been allocated.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

- **[[74.07]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.07
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 74.01 to 74.06 do not apply
  to a person who prints or publishes or otherwise disseminates a
  representation, including an advertisement, on behalf of another
  person in Canada, where the person establishes that the person
  obtained and recorded the name and address of that other person and
  accepted the representation in good faith for printing, publishing or
  other dissemination in the ordinary course of that person's business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 74.01 to 74.06 do not apply in respect of
  conduct prohibited by sections 52.1, 53, 55 and 55.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2002, c. 16, s. 9
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil rights not affected

**[[74.08]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.08 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
provided in this Part, nothing in this Part shall be construed as
depriving any person of a civil right of action.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

### [Administrative Remedies]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89299 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[74.09]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.09 .sectionLabel}** In sections 74.1 to
74.14 and 74.18, [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an application by the Commissioner, the
  Tribunal, the Federal Court or the superior court of a province; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of an application made by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2002, c. 8, s. 183
- [2024, c. 15, s. 238](#1462813-1531901){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462813-1531901.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74.09-20030702.html "Link to previous version of section 74.09"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of reviewable conduct and
judicial order

- **[[74.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner or a person granted leave under section 103.1, a court
  determines that a person is engaging in or has engaged in reviewable
  conduct under this Part, the court may order the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} not to engage in the conduct or substantially
    similar reviewable conduct;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to publish or otherwise disseminate a notice, in
    such manner and at such times as the court may specify, to bring to
    the attention of the class of persons likely to have been reached or
    affected by the conduct, the name under which the person carries on
    business and the determination made under this section, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a description of the reviewable conduct,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the time period and geographical area to which
      the conduct relates, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a description of the manner in which any
      representation or advertisement was disseminated, including, where
      applicable, the name of the publication or other medium employed;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to pay an administrative monetary penalty, in any
    manner that the court specifies, in an amount not exceeding

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an individual, the greater of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} \$750,000 and, for each subsequent order,
        \$1,000,000, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} three times the value of the benefit derived
        from the deceptive conduct, if that amount can be reasonably
        determined, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a corporation, the greater of

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} \$10,000,000 and, for each subsequent order,
        \$15,000,000, and

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} three times the value of the benefit derived
        from the deceptive conduct, or, if that amount cannot be
        reasonably determined, 3% of the corporation's annual worldwide
        gross revenues; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of conduct that is reviewable under
    paragraph 74.01(1)(a), to pay an amount, not exceeding the total of
    the amounts paid to the person for the products in respect of which
    the conduct was engaged in, to be distributed among the persons to
    whom the products were sold --- except wholesalers, retailers or
    other distributors, to the extent that they have resold or
    distributed the products --- in any manner that the court considers
    appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An order made under paragraph (1)(a) applies for a
  period of ten years unless the court specifies a shorter period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No order may be made against a person under paragraph
  (1)(b), (c) or (d) if the person establishes that the person exercised
  due diligence to prevent the reviewable conduct from occurring.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The terms of an order made against a person under
  paragraph (1)(b), (c) or (d) shall be determined with a view to
  promoting conduct by that person that is in conformity with the
  purposes of this Part and not with a view to punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating or mitigating factors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any evidence of the following shall be taken into
  account in determining the amount of an administrative monetary
  penalty under paragraph (1)(c):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the reach of the conduct within the relevant
    geographic market;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the frequency and duration of the conduct;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vulnerability of the class of persons likely to
    be adversely affected by the conduct;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the materiality of any representation;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the likelihood of self-correction in the relevant
    geographic market;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the effect on competition in the relevant market;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenue from sales affected by the
    conduct;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the financial position of the person against whom
    the order is made;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the history of compliance with this Act by the
    person against whom the order is made;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} any decision of the court in relation to an
    application for an order under paragraph (1)(d);

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} any other amounts paid or ordered to be paid by the
    person against whom the order is made as a refund or as restitution
    or other compensation in respect of the conduct; and

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of subsequent order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(c), an order made
  against a person in respect of conduct that is reviewable under
  paragraph 74.01(1)(a), (b), (b.1) or (c), subsection 74.01(2) or (3)
  or section 74.011, 74.02, 74.04, 74.05 or 74.06 is a subsequent order
  if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order was previously made against the person
    under this section in respect of conduct reviewable under the same
    provision;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person was previously convicted of an offence
    under the provision of Part VI, as that Part read immediately before
    the coming into force of this Part, that corresponded to the
    provision of this Part;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an order in respect of conduct
    reviewable under paragraph 74.01(1)(a) or section 74.011, the person
    was previously convicted of an offence under section 52, or under
    paragraph 52(1)(a) as it read immediately before the coming into
    force of this Part; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an order in respect of conduct
    reviewable under subsection 74.01(2) or (3), the person was
    previously convicted of an offence under paragraph 52(1)(d) as it
    read immediately before the coming into force of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amounts already paid

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In determining an amount to be paid under paragraph
  (1)(d), the court shall take into account any other amounts paid or
  ordered to be paid by the person against whom the order is made as a
  refund or as restitution or other compensation in respect of the
  products.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of the order

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The court may specify in an order made under
  paragraph (1)(d) any terms that it considers necessary for the order's
  implementation, including terms

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} specifying how the payment is to be administered;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the appointment of an administrator to
    administer the payment and specifying the terms of administration;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} requiring the person against whom the order is made
    to pay the administrative costs related to the payment as well as
    the fees to be paid to an administrator;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} requiring that potential claimants be notified in
    the time and manner specified by the court;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} specifying the time and manner for making claims;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} specifying the conditions for the eligibility of
    claimants, including conditions relating to the return of the
    products to the person against whom the order is made; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} providing for the manner in which, and the terms on
    which, any amount of the payment that remains unclaimed or
    undistributed is to be dealt with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of terms

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Commissioner or the person
  against whom the order is made, the court may vary any term that is
  specified under subsection (8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inferences

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} In considering an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal may not draw any inference
  from the fact that the Commissioner has or has not taken any action in
  respect of the matter raised by the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2009, c. 2, s. 424
- [2022, c. 10, s. 260](#1357336-1366447){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357336-1366447.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 239](#1462815-1484062){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462815-1484062.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-74.1-20240620.html "Link to previous version of section 74.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deduction from administrative monetary
penalty

- **[[74.101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.101
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a court determines that a person
  is engaging in or has engaged in conduct that is reviewable under
  section 74.011 and orders the person to pay an administrative monetary
  penalty under paragraph 74.1(1)(c), then the court shall deduct from
  the amount of the penalty that it determines any amount that the
  person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has been ordered to pay under paragraph 51(1)(b) of
    [An Act to promote the efficiency and adaptability of the Canadian
    economy by regulating certain activities that discourage reliance on
    electronic means of carrying out commercial activities, and to amend
    the Canadian Radio-television and Telecommunications Commission Act,
    the Competition Act, the Personal Information Protection and
    Electronic Documents Act and the Telecommunications
    Act](/eng/acts/E-1.6) in respect of the same conduct; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has agreed in a settlement agreement to pay on
    account of amounts referred to in paragraph 51(1)(b) of that Act in
    respect of the same conduct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restitution and interim injunction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a court determines that a person is engaging in or
  has engaged in conduct that is reviewable under subsection 74.011(2),
  it may order the person to pay an amount under paragraph 74.1(1)(d),
  and may issue an interim injunction under section 74.111, as if the
  conduct were conduct that is reviewable under paragraph 74.01(1)(a).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2010, c. 23, s. 79
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary order

- **[[74.11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.11
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Commissioner
  or a person granted leave under section 103.1, a court may order a
  person who it appears to the court is engaging in conduct that is
  reviewable under this Part not to engage in that conduct or
  substantially similar reviewable conduct if it appears to the court
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} serious harm is likely to ensue unless the order is
    issued; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the balance of convenience favours issuing the
    order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary order --- supply of a product

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Commissioner or a person
  granted leave under section 103.1, a court may order any person named
  in the application to refrain from supplying to another person a
  product that it appears to the court is or is likely to be used to
  engage in conduct that is reviewable under this Part, or to do any act
  or thing that it appears to the court could prevent a person from
  engaging in such conduct, if it appears to the court that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} serious harm is likely to ensue unless the order is
    issued; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the balance of convenience favours issuing the
    order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), an order made under
  subsection (1) or (1.1) has effect, or may be extended on application
  by the Commissioner or a person granted leave under section 103.1, for
  any period that the court considers sufficient to meet the
  circumstances of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), at least 48 hours' notice
  of an application referred to in subsection (1), (1.1) or (2) must be
  given by or on behalf of the Commissioner or the person granted leave
  under section 103.1 to the person in respect of whom the order or
  extension is sought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
  application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The court may proceed [ex parte]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} with an application made by the Commissioner under
  subsection (1) or (1.1) if it is satisfied that subsection (3) cannot
  reasonably be complied with or that the urgency of the situation is
  such that service of notice in accordance with subsection (3) would
  not be in the public interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of [ex parte]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An order issued [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} as
  the result of an application made by the Commissioner under
  subsection (1) or (1.1) has effect for the period that is specified in
  it, not exceeding seven days unless, on further application made by
  the Commissioner on notice as provided in subsection (3), the court
  extends the order for any additional period that it considers
  necessary and sufficient.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of Commissioner

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If an order issued under this section as the result
  of an application made by the Commissioner under subsection (1) or
  (1.1) is in effect, the Commissioner must proceed as expeditiously as
  possible to complete the inquiry under section 10 arising out of the
  conduct in respect of which the order was issued.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2002, c. 16, s. 10
- 2010, c. 23, s. 80
- [2024, c. 15, s. 240](#1462824-1531924){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462824-1531924.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74.11-20140701.html "Link to previous version of section 74.11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim injunction

- **[[74.111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.111
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner or a person granted leave under section 103.1, a court
  finds a strong [prima facie]{.otherLang lang="la"} case that a person
  is engaging in or has engaged in conduct that is reviewable under
  paragraph 74.01(1)(a), and the court is satisfied that the person owns
  or has possession or control of articles within the jurisdiction of
  the court and is disposing of or is likely to dispose of them by any
  means, and that the disposal of the articles will substantially impair
  the enforceability of an order made under paragraph 74.1(1)(d), the
  court may issue an interim injunction forbidding the person or any
  other person from disposing of or otherwise dealing with the articles,
  other than in the manner and on the terms specified in the injunction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement to be included

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any application for an injunction under
  subsection (1) must include a statement that the Commissioner or the
  person granted leave under section 103.1 has applied for an order
  under paragraph 74.1(1)(d), or that the Commissioner or the person
  intends to apply for an order under that paragraph if the Commissioner
  or the person applies for an order under paragraph 74.1(1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (6), the injunction has effect,
  or may be extended on application by the Commissioner or the person
  granted leave under section 103.1, for any period that the court
  considers sufficient to meet the circumstances of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of application

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), at least 48 hours' notice
  of an application referred to in subsection (1) or (3) must be given
  by or on behalf of the Commissioner or the person granted leave under
  section 103.1 to the person in respect of whom the injunction or
  extension is sought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
  application

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The court may proceed [ex parte]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} with an application made by the Commissioner under
  subsection (1) if it is satisfied that subsection (4) cannot
  reasonably be complied with or that the urgency of the situation is
  such that service of the notice in accordance with subsection (4)
  might defeat the purpose of the injunction or would otherwise not be
  in the public interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of [ex parte]{.otherLang
  lang="la"} injunction

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An injunction issued [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
  as the result of an application made by the Commissioner under
  subsection (1) has effect for the period that is specified in it, not
  exceeding seven days unless, on further application made by the
  Commissioner on notice as provided in subsection (4), the court
  extends the injunction for any additional period that it considers
  sufficient.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submissions to set aside

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} On application of the person against whom an [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} injunction is made, the court may make an
  order setting aside the injunction or varying it subject to any
  conditions that it considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of Commissioner

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If an injunction issued under this section as the
  result of an application made by the Commissioner under subsection (1)
  is in effect, the Commissioner must proceed as expeditiously as
  possible to complete any inquiry under section 10 arising out of the
  conduct in respect of which the injunction was issued.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in this section.

  [[dispose]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[dispose]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to an article,
      includes removing it from the jurisdiction of the court, depleting
      its value, leasing it to another person or creating any security
      interest in it. ([disposer]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[security interest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[security interest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any interest or
      right in property that secures payment or performance of an
      obligation and includes an interest or right created by or arising
      out of a debenture, mortgage, hypothec, lien, pledge, charge,
      security, deemed or actual trust, assignment or encumbrance of any
      kind whatever, however or whenever arising, created, deemed to
      arise or otherwise provided for. ([garantie]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 2009, c. 2, s. 425
- [2024, c. 15, s. 241](#1462826-1531938){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462826-1531938.html"}
:::

Version](section-74.111-20090312.html "Link to previous version of section 74.111"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent agreement

- **[[74.12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.12
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner and a person in
  respect of whom the Commissioner has applied or may apply for an order
  under this Part may sign a consent agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of consent agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement shall be based on terms that
  could be the subject of an order of a court against that person, and
  may include other terms, whether or not they could be imposed by the
  court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement may be filed with the court for
  immediate registration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of registration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Upon registration of the consent agreement, the
  proceedings, if any, are terminated and the consent agreement has the
  same force and effect, and proceedings may be taken, as if it were an
  order of the court.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2002, c. 16, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with consent agreement

- **[[74.121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.121
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner, the court determines that a person, without good and
  sufficient cause, the proof of which lies on the person, has failed to
  comply or is likely to fail to comply with a consent agreement
  registered under subsection 74.12(3), the court may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibit the person from doing anything that, in
    the court's opinion, may constitute a failure to comply with the
    agreement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order the person to take any action that is
    necessary to comply with the agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order the person to pay, in any manner that the
    court specifies, an administrative monetary penalty in an amount not
    exceeding \$10,000 for each day on which they fail to comply with
    the agreement, determined by the court after taking into account any
    evidence of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person's financial position,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's history of compliance with this
      Act,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the period of non-compliance,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} grant any other relief that the court considers
    appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The terms of an order under paragraph (1)(c) are to
  be determined with a view to promoting conduct by the person that is
  in conformity with the purposes of this Act and not with a view to
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrative monetary penalty imposed under
  paragraph (1)(c) is a debt due to His Majesty in right of Canada and
  may be recovered as such from the person in a court of competent
  jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 242](#1462831-1484079){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462831-1484079.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rescission or variation of consent
agreement or order

**[[74.13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.13 .sectionLabel}** The court may
rescind or vary a consent agreement that it has registered or an order
that it has made under this Part, on application by the Commissioner or
the person who consented to the agreement, or the person against whom
the order was made, if the court finds that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances that led to the making of the
  agreement or order have changed and, in the circumstances that exist
  at the time the application is made, the agreement or order would not
  have been made or would have been ineffective in achieving its
  intended purpose; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Commissioner and the person who consented to the
  agreement have consented to an alternative agreement or the
  Commissioner and the person against whom the order was made have
  consented to an alternative order.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2002, c. 16, s. 11
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent agreement --- parties to a
private action

- **[[74.131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.131
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a person granted leave under
  section 103.1 makes an application to the Tribunal for an order under
  section 74.1 and the terms of the order are agreed to by the person in
  respect of whom the order is sought and consistent with the provisions
  of this Act, a consent agreement may be filed with the Tribunal for
  registration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Commissioner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On filing the consent agreement with the Tribunal for
  registration, the parties shall serve a copy of it on the Commissioner
  without delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement must be published without delay
  in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement must be registered within 30
  days after its publication unless a third party makes an application
  to the Tribunal before then to cancel the agreement or replace it with
  an order of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of registration

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On registration, the consent agreement has the same
  force and effect, and proceedings may be taken, as if it were an order
  of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner may intervene

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Commissioner, the Tribunal may
  vary or rescind a registered consent agreement if it finds that the
  agreement is not in conformity with the purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner must give notice of an application
  under subsection (6) to the parties to the consent agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 243](#1462833-1531955){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462833-1531955.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with consent agreement

- **[[74.132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.132
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner, the Tribunal determines that a person, without good and
  sufficient cause, the proof of which lies on the person, has failed to
  comply or is likely to fail to comply with a consent agreement
  registered under subsection 74.131(4), the Tribunal may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibit the person from doing anything that, in
    the Tribunal's opinion, may constitute a failure to comply with the
    agreement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order the person to take any action that is
    necessary to comply with the agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order the person to pay, in any manner that the
    Tribunal specifies, an administrative monetary penalty in an amount
    not exceeding \$10,000 for each day on which they fail to comply
    with the agreement, determined by the Tribunal after taking into
    account any evidence of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person's financial position,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's history of compliance with this
      Act,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the period of non-compliance,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} grant any other relief that the Tribunal considers
    appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The terms of an order under paragraph (1)(c) are to
  be determined with a view to promoting conduct by the person that is
  in conformity with the purposes of this Act and not with a view to
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrative monetary penalty imposed under
  paragraph (1)(c) is a debt due to His Majesty in right of Canada and
  may be recovered as such from the person in a court of competent
  jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 243](#1462833-1531972){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462833-1531972.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of agreement on Commissioner

- **[[74.133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.133
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a person granted leave under
  section 103.1 makes an application to the Tribunal for an order under
  section 74.1 and the person discontinues the application by reason of
  having entered into an agreement with any other person, the parties to
  the agreement must serve a copy of it on the Commissioner within 10
  days after the day on which it is entered into.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner may intervene

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Commissioner, the Tribunal may
  vary or rescind the agreement if it finds that the agreement is not in
  conformity with the purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner must give notice of an application
  under subsection (2) to the parties to the agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 243](#1462833-1531981){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462833-1531981.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to serve

- **[[74.134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.134
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner, the Tribunal determines that a person, without good and
  sufficient cause, the proof of which lies on the person, has failed to
  serve a copy of an agreement on the Commissioner in accordance with
  subsection 74.133(1), the Tribunal may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order the person to serve the Commissioner with a
    copy of the agreement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issue an interim order prohibiting any person from
    doing anything that, in the Tribunal's opinion, may constitute or be
    directed toward the implementation of the agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order the person to pay, in any manner that the
    Tribunal specifies, an administrative monetary penalty in an amount
    not exceeding \$10,000 for each day on which they fail to serve a
    copy of the agreement on the Commissioner, determined by the
    Tribunal after taking into account any evidence of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person's financial position,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's history of compliance with this
      Act,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the period of non-compliance,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} grant any other relief that the Tribunal considers
    appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The terms of an order under paragraph (1)(c) are to
  be determined with a view to promoting conduct by the person that is
  in conformity with the purposes of this Act and not with a view to
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrative monetary penalty imposed under
  paragraph (1)(c) is a debt due to His Majesty in right of Canada and
  may be recovered as such from the person in a court of competent
  jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 243](#1462833-1531998){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462833-1531998.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

**[[74.14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.14 .sectionLabel}** In determining
whether or not to make an order under this Part, the court shall not
exclude from consideration any evidence by reason only that it might be
evidence in respect of an offence under this Act or in respect of which
another order could be made by the court under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

**[[74.15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.15 .sectionLabel}** The amount of an
administrative monetary penalty imposed on a person under paragraph
74.1(1)(c) is a debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada and may be
recovered as such from that person in a court of competent jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where proceedings commenced under section
52 or 52.01

**[[74.16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.16 .sectionLabel}** No application may
be made under this Part against a person on the basis of facts that are
the same or substantially the same as the facts on the basis of which
proceedings have been commenced against that person under section 52 or
52.01.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2010, c. 23, s. 81
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74.16-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 74.16"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Rules of Procedure]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89439 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power of courts

**[[74.17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.17 .sectionLabel}** The rules committee
of the Federal Court, or a superior court of a province, may make rules
respecting the procedure for the disposition of applications by that
court under this Part.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

### [Appeals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89444 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to Federal Court of Appeal

- **[[74.18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.18
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An appeal may be brought in the
  Federal Court of Appeal from any decision or order made under this
  Part, or from a refusal to make an order, by the Tribunal or the
  Federal Court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal to provincial court of appeal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An appeal may be brought in the court of appeal of a
  province from any decision or order made under this Part, or from a
  refusal to make an order, by a superior court of the province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition of appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Federal Court of Appeal or the court of
  appeal of the province allows an appeal under this section, it may
  quash the decision or order appealed from, refer the matter back to
  the court appealed from or make any decision or order that, in its
  opinion, that court should have made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
- 2002, c. 8, s. 183
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-74.18-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 74.18"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal on question of fact

**[[74.19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74.19 .sectionLabel}** An appeal on a
question of fact from a decision or order made under this Part may be
brought only with the leave of the Federal Court of Appeal or the court
of appeal of the province, as the case may be.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 2, s. 22
:::

## [PART VIII]{.HLabel1}[Matters Reviewable by Tribunal]{.HTitleText1} {#h-89458 .Part}

### [Restrictive Trade Practices]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89459 .Subheading}

#### [Refusal to Deal]{.HTitleText3} {#h-89460 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Jurisdiction of Tribunal --- cases of
refusal to deal

- **[[75]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-75 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Tribunal may, on application by the Commissioner or a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, order one or more suppliers of a product,
  including a means of diagnosis or repair, in a market to accept a
  person as a customer, or to make the means of diagnosis or repair
  available to a person, within a specified period and on the terms that
  the Tribunal considers appropriate if the Tribunal finds that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person is substantially affected in the whole
    or part of their business or is precluded from carrying on business
    due to their inability to obtain adequate supplies of the product
    anywhere in the market on usual trade terms;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person is unable to obtain adequate supplies of
    the product because of insufficient competition among suppliers of
    the product in the market;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the person is willing and able to meet the usual
    trade terms of the supplier or suppliers of the product;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the product is in ample supply or, in the case of a
    means of diagnosis or repair, can be readily supplied; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the refusal to deal is having or is likely to have
    an adverse effect on competition in a market.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} An order made under subsection (1) does not apply
  in the case of an article if, within the specified time, any customs
  duties on the article are removed, reduced or remitted and the effect
  of the removal, reduction or remission is to place the person on an
  equal footing with other persons who are able to obtain adequate
  supplies of the article in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional order --- person granted
  leave

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal makes an order under subsection (1)
  as the result of an application by a person granted leave under
  section 103.1, it may also order any supplier in respect of whom the
  order applies to pay an amount, not exceeding the value of the benefit
  derived from the conduct that is the subject of the order, to be
  distributed among the applicant and any other person affected by the
  conduct, in any manner that the Tribunal considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of the order

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may specify in an order made under
  subsection (1.2) any term that it considers necessary for the order's
  implementation, including a term

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} specifying how the payment is to be administered;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the appointment of an administrator to
    administer the payment and specifying the terms of administration;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} requiring the person against whom the order is made
    to pay the administrative costs related to the payment as well as
    the fees to be paid to an administrator;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} requiring that potential claimants be notified in
    the time and manner specified by the Tribunal;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} specifying the time and manner for making claims;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} specifying the conditions for the eligibility of
    claimants, including conditions relating to the return of the
    products to the person against whom the order is made; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} providing for the manner in which, and the terms on
    which, any amount of the payment that remains unclaimed or
    undistributed is to be dealt with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When article is a separate product

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, an article is not a
  separate product in a market only because it is differentiated from
  other articles in its class by a trademark, proprietary name or the
  like, unless the article so differentiated occupies such a dominant
  position in that market as to substantially affect the ability of a
  person to carry on business in that class of articles unless that
  person has access to the article so differentiated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trade secrets

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section is to be interpreted as
  requiring the disclosure of any information that is a trade secret.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The following definitions apply in this section.

  [[means of diagnosis or repair]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[means of diagnosis or repair]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes
      diagnostic, maintenance, repair and calibration information,
      technical updates, diagnostic software or tools and any related
      documentation and service parts. ([moyen de diagnostic ou de
      réparation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[trade terms]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[trade terms]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means terms in respect of
      payment, units of purchase and reasonable technical and servicing
      requirements. ([conditions de commerce]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inferences

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In considering an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal may not draw any inference
  from the fact that the Commissioner has or has not taken any action in
  respect of the matter raised by the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 75
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- 2002, c. 16, s. 11.1
- [2014, c. 20, s. 366(E)](#1034903-1145652){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1034903-1145652.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 244](#1462835-1484095){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462835-1484095.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-75-20240620.html "Link to previous version of section 75"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Price Maintenance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-89478 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Price maintenance

- **[[76]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-76 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by the Commissioner or a person granted leave under
  section 103.1, the Tribunal may make an order under subsection (2) if
  the Tribunal finds that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person referred to in subsection (3) directly or
    indirectly

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by agreement, threat, promise or any like means,
      has influenced upward, or has discouraged the reduction of, the
      price at which the person's customer or any other person to whom
      the product comes for resale supplies or offers to supply or
      advertises a product within Canada, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} has refused to supply a product to or has
      otherwise discriminated against any person or class of persons
      engaged in business in Canada because of the low pricing policy of
      that other person or class of persons; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the conduct has had, is having or is likely to have
    an adverse effect on competition in a market.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may make an order prohibiting the person
  referred to in subsection (3) from continuing to engage in the conduct
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or requiring them to accept another
  person as a customer within a specified time on usual trade terms.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons subject to order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An order may be made under subsection (2) against a
  person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is engaged in the business of producing or
    supplying a product;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} extends credit by way of credit cards or is
    otherwise engaged in a business that relates to credit cards; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has the exclusive rights and privileges conferred
    by a patent, certificate of supplementary protection issued under
    the [Patent Act](/eng/acts/P-4), trademark, copyright, registered
    industrial design or registered integrated circuit topography.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When no order may be made

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No order may be made under subsection (2) if the
  person referred to in subsection (3) and the customer or other person
  referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(i) or (ii) are

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} principal and agent or mandator and mandatary;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an entity and an individual who controls it or
    affiliated entities; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} directors, agents, mandataries, officers or
    employees of the same entity or of entities that are affiliated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suggested retail price

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, a suggestion by a
  producer or supplier of a product of a resale price or minimum resale
  price for the product, however arrived at, is proof that the person to
  whom the suggestion is made is influenced in accordance with the
  suggestion, in the absence of proof that the producer or supplier, in
  so doing, also made it clear to the person that they were under no
  obligation to accept the suggestion and would in no way suffer in
  their business relations with the producer or supplier or with any
  other person if they failed to accept the suggestion.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advertised price

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, the publication by
  a producer or supplier of a product, other than a retailer, of an
  advertisement that mentions a resale price for the product is proof
  that the producer or supplier is influencing upward the selling price
  of any person to whom the product comes for resale, unless the price
  is expressed in a way that makes it clear to any person whose
  attention the advertisement comes to that the product may be sold at a
  lower price.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (5) and (6) do not apply to a price that
  is affixed or applied to a product or its package or container.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to supply

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the Commissioner or a person
  granted leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal finds that any person,
  by agreement, threat, promise or any like means, has induced a
  supplier, whether within or outside Canada, as a condition of doing
  business with the supplier, to refuse to supply a product to a
  particular person or class of persons because of the low pricing
  policy of that person or class of persons, and that the conduct of
  inducement has had, is having or is likely to have an adverse effect
  on competition in a market, the Tribunal may make an order prohibiting
  the person from continuing to engage in the conduct or requiring the
  person to do business with the supplier on usual trade terms.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no order may be made

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} No order may be made under subsection (2) in respect
  of conduct referred to in subparagraph (1)(a)(ii) if the Tribunal is
  satisfied that the person or class of persons referred to in that
  subparagraph, in respect of products supplied by the person referred
  to in subsection (3),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was making a practice of using the products as loss
    leaders, that is to say, not for the purpose of making a profit on
    those products but for purposes of advertising;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was making a practice of using the products not for
    the purpose of selling them at a profit but for the purpose of
    attracting customers in the hope of selling them other products;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} was making a practice of engaging in misleading
    advertising; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} made a practice of not providing the level of
    servicing that purchasers of the products might reasonably expect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inferences

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} In considering an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal may not draw any inference
  from the fact that the Commissioner has or has not taken any action in
  respect of the matter raised by the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where proceedings commenced under
  section 45, 49, 79 or 90.1

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made under this section
  against a person on the basis of facts that are the same or
  substantially the same as the facts on the basis of which

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} proceedings have been commenced against that person
    under section 45 or 49; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order against that person has been made under
    section 79 or 90.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional order --- person granted
  leave

  [(11.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal makes an order under
  subsection (2) as the result of an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, it may also order the person against whom
  the order is made to pay an amount, not exceeding the value of the
  benefit derived from the conduct that is the subject of the order, to
  be distributed among the applicant and any other person affected by
  the conduct, in any manner that the Tribunal considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of the order

  [(11.2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may specify in an order made under
  subsection (11.1) any term that it considers necessary for the order's
  implementation, including any term referred to in any of paragraphs
  75(1.3)(a) to (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[trade
  terms]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this section, [[trade
  terms]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means terms in respect of payment, units
  of purchase and reasonable technical and servicing requirements.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 76
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- 2009, c. 2, s. 426
- 2014, c. 20, s. 366(E)
- 2017, c. 6, s. 124
- 2018, c. 8, s. 112

#### [Exclusive Dealing, Tied Selling and Market Restriction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-89522 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[77]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-77 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section,

  [[exclusive dealing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[exclusive dealing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any practice whereby a supplier of a product,
        as a condition of supplying the product to a customer, requires
        that customer to

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} deal only or primarily in products supplied
          by or designated by the supplier or the supplier's nominee, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} refrain from dealing in a specified class or
          kind of product except as supplied by the supplier or the
          nominee, and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any practice whereby a supplier of a product
        induces a customer to meet a condition set out in subparagraph
        (a)(i) or (ii) by offering to supply the product to the customer
        on more favourable terms or conditions if the customer agrees to
        meet the condition set out in either of those
        subparagraphs; ([exclusivité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[market restriction]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[market restriction]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any practice
      whereby a supplier of a product, as a condition of supplying the
      product to a customer, requires that customer to supply any
      product only in a defined market, or exacts a penalty of any kind
      from the customer if he supplies any product outside a defined
      market; ([limitation du marché]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[tied selling]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[tied selling]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any practice whereby a supplier of a product,
        as a condition of supplying the product (the "tying" product) to
        a customer, requires that customer to

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} acquire any other product from the supplier
          or the supplier's nominee, or

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} refrain from using or distributing, in
          conjunction with the tying product, another product that is
          not of a brand or manufacture designated by the supplier or
          the nominee, and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any practice whereby a supplier of a product
        induces a customer to meet a condition set out in subparagraph
        (a)(i) or (ii) by offering to supply the tying product to the
        customer on more favourable terms or conditions if the customer
        agrees to meet the condition set out in either of those
        subparagraphs. ([ventes liées]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exclusive dealing and tied selling

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, on application by the Commissioner or a person
  granted leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal finds that exclusive
  dealing or tied selling, because it is engaged in by a major supplier
  of a product in a market or because it is widespread in a market, is
  likely to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} impede entry into or expansion of a firm in a
    market,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} impede introduction of a product into or expansion
    of sales of a product in a market, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} have any other exclusionary effect in a market,

  with the result that competition is or is likely to be lessened
  substantially, the Tribunal may make an order directed to all or any
  of the suppliers against whom an order is sought prohibiting them from
  continuing to engage in that exclusive dealing or tied selling and
  containing any other requirement that, in its opinion, is necessary to
  overcome the effects thereof in the market or to restore or stimulate
  competition in the market.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Market restriction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where, on application by the Commissioner or a person
  granted leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal finds that market
  restriction, because it is engaged in by a major supplier of a product
  or because it is widespread in relation to a product, is likely to
  substantially lessen competition in relation to the product, the
  Tribunal may make an order directed to all or any of the suppliers
  against whom an order is sought prohibiting them from continuing to
  engage in market restriction and containing any other requirement
  that, in its opinion, is necessary to restore or stimulate competition
  in relation to the product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional order --- person granted
  leave

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal makes an order under subsection (2)
  or (3) as the result of an application by a person granted leave under
  section 103.1, it may also order any supplier in respect of whom the
  order applies to pay an amount, not exceeding the value of the benefit
  derived from the conduct that is the subject of the order, to be
  distributed among the applicant and any other person affected by the
  conduct, in any manner that the Tribunal considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of the order

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may specify in an order made under
  subsection (3.1) any term that it considers necessary for the order's
  implementation, including any term referred to in any of paragraphs
  75(1.3)(a) to (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where no order to be made and
  limitation on application of order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall not make an order under this
  section where, in its opinion,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exclusive dealing or market restriction is or will
    be engaged in only for a reasonable period of time to facilitate
    entry of a new supplier of a product into a market or of a new
    product into a market,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} tied selling that is engaged in is reasonable
    having regard to the technological relationship between or among the
    products to which it applies, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} tied selling that is engaged in by a person in the
    business of lending money is for the purpose of better securing
    loans made by that person and is reasonably necessary for that
    purpose.

  No order made under this section applies in respect of exclusive
  dealing, market restriction or tied selling between or among entities
  that are affiliated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If entity affiliated

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (4), in addition to
  the circumstances specified in paragraph 2(2)(a) or (b) under which
  two entities are affiliated, an entity is affiliated with another
  entity in respect of any agreement between them in which one of them
  grants to the other the right to use a trademark or trade name to
  identify the business of the grantee, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the business is related to the sale or
    distribution, in accordance with a marketing plan or system
    prescribed substantially by the grantor, of a multiplicity of
    products obtained from competing sources of supply and a
    multiplicity of suppliers; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no one product dominates the business.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When persons deemed to be affiliated

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (4) in its application
  to market restriction, where there is an agreement whereby one person
  (the "first" person) supplies or causes to be supplied to another
  person (the "second" person) an ingredient or ingredients that the
  second person processes by the addition of labour and material into an
  article of food or drink that he then sells in association with a
  trademark that the first person owns or in respect of which the first
  person is a registered user, the first person and the second person
  are deemed, in respect of the agreement, to be affiliated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inferences

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In considering an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal may not draw any inference
  from the fact that the Commissioner has or has not taken any action in
  respect of the matter raised by the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. C-34, s. 77
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, ss. 23, 37, c. 31, s. 52(F)
- 2002, c. 16, ss. 11.2, 11.3
- 2014, c. 20, s. 366(E)
- 2018, c. 8, s. 113
- [2024, c. 15, s. 246](#1462847-1532018){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462847-1532018.html"}
:::
