#### [Abuse of Dominant Position]{.HTitleText3} {#h-89563 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[anti-competitive
act]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[78]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-78 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 79, [[anti-competitive
  act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any act intended to have a predatory,
  exclusionary or disciplinary negative effect on a competitor, or to
  have an adverse effect on competition, and includes any of the
  following acts:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} squeezing, by a vertically integrated supplier, of
    the margin available to an unintegrated customer who competes with
    the supplier, for the purpose of impeding or preventing the
    customer's entry into, or expansion in, a market;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} acquisition by a supplier of a customer who would
    otherwise be available to a competitor of the supplier, or
    acquisition by a customer of a supplier who would otherwise be
    available to a competitor of the customer, for the purpose of
    impeding or preventing the competitor's entry into, or eliminating
    the competitor from, a market;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} freight equalization on the plant of a competitor
    for the purpose of impeding or preventing the competitor's entry
    into, or eliminating the competitor from, a market;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} use of fighting brands introduced selectively on a
    temporary basis to discipline or eliminate a competitor;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} pre-emption of scarce facilities or resources
    required by a competitor for the operation of a business, with the
    object of withholding the facilities or resources from a market;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} buying up of products to prevent the erosion of
    existing price levels;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} adoption of product specifications that are
    incompatible with products produced by any other person and are
    designed to prevent his entry into, or to eliminate him from, a
    market;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} requiring or inducing a supplier to sell only or
    primarily to certain customers, or to refrain from selling to a
    competitor, with the object of preventing a competitor's entry into,
    or expansion in, a market;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} selling articles at a price lower than the
    acquisition cost for the purpose of disciplining or eliminating a
    competitor;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} a selective or discriminatory response to an actual
    or potential competitor for the purpose of impeding or preventing
    the competitor's entry into, or expansion in, a market or
    eliminating the competitor from a market; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} directly or indirectly imposing excessive and
    unfair selling prices.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2009, c. 2, s. 427\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 2000, c. 15, s. 13
- 2009, c. 2, s. 427
- [2022, c. 10, s. 261](#1357338-1366453){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357338-1366453.html"}
- [2023, c. 31, s. 7.1](#1431663-1432213){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1431663-1432213.html"}
:::

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition if abuse of dominant position

- **[[79]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by the Commissioner or a person granted leave under
  section 103.1, if the Tribunal finds that one or more persons
  substantially or completely control a class or species of business
  throughout Canada or any area of Canada, it may make an order
  prohibiting the person or persons from engaging in a practice or
  conduct if it finds that the person or persons have engaged in or are
  engaging in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a practice of anti-competitive acts; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} that had, is having or is likely to have the
      effect of preventing or lessening competition substantially in a
      market in which the person or persons have a plausible competitive
      interest, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the effect is not a result of superior
      competitive performance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional or alternative order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, on an application under subsection (1), the
  Tribunal finds that a practice of anti-competitive acts amounts to
  conduct that has had or is having the effect of preventing or
  lessening competition substantially in a market in which the person or
  persons have a plausible competitive interest and that an order under
  subsection (1) is not likely to restore competition in that market,
  the Tribunal may, in addition to or in lieu of making an order under
  subsection (1), make an order directing any or all persons against
  whom an order is sought to take actions, including the divestiture of
  assets or shares, that are reasonable and necessary to overcome the
  effects of the practice in that market.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In making an order under subsection (2), the Tribunal
  shall make the order in such terms as will in its opinion interfere
  with the rights of any person to whom the order is directed or any
  other person affected by it only to the extent necessary to achieve
  the purpose of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrative monetary penalty

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal finds that a person has engaged in
  or is engaging in a practice of anti-competitive acts that amounts to
  conduct that has had or is having the effect of preventing or
  lessening competition substantially in a market in which the person
  has a plausible competitive interest and it makes an order against the
  person under subsection (1) or (2), it may also order them to pay, in
  any manner that it specifies, an administrative monetary penalty in an
  amount not exceeding the greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} \$25,000,000 and, for each subsequent order under
    either of those subsections, an amount not exceeding \$35,000,000,
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} three times the value of the benefit derived from
    the anti-competitive practice, or, if that amount cannot be
    reasonably determined, 3% of the person's annual worldwide gross
    revenues.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating or mitigating factors

  [(3.2)]{.lawlabel} In determining the amount of an administrative
  monetary penalty, the Tribunal shall take into account any evidence of
  the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the effect on competition in the relevant market;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenue from sales affected by the
    practice;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any actual or anticipated profits affected by the
    practice;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the financial position of the person against whom
    the order is made;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} the amount that the person against whom the order
    is made is required to pay under an order made under subsection
    (4.1);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the history of compliance with this Act by the
    person against whom the order is made; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(3.3)]{.lawlabel} The purpose of an order made against a person under
  subsection (3.1) is to promote practices by that person that are in
  conformity with the purposes of this section and not to punish that
  person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In determining, for the purposes of subsections (1)
  and (2), whether conduct has had, is having or is likely to have the
  effect of preventing or lessening competition substantially in a
  market, the Tribunal may consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the effect of the conduct on barriers to entry in
    the market, including network effects;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the effect of the conduct on price or non-price
    competition, including quality, choice or consumer privacy;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the nature and extent of change and innovation in a
    relevant market; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that is relevant to competition in
    the market that is or would be affected by the conduct.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional order --- person granted
  leave

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} If, as the result of an application by a person
  granted leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal finds that a person
  has engaged in or is engaging in a practice of anti-competitive acts
  that amounts to conduct that has had or is having the effect of
  preventing or lessening competition substantially in a market in which
  the person has a plausible competitive interest and it makes an order
  under subsection (1) or (2) against the person, it may also order the
  person against whom the order is made to pay an amount, not exceeding
  the value of the benefit derived from the conduct that is the subject
  of the order, to be distributed among the applicant and any other
  person affected by the conduct, in any manner that the Tribunal
  considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of the order

  [(4.2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may specify in an order made under
  subsection (4.1) any term that it considers necessary for the order's
  implementation, including any term referred to in any of paragraphs
  75(1.3)(a) to (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, an act engaged in
  pursuant only to the exercise of any right or enjoyment of any
  interest derived under the [Copyright Act](/eng/acts/C-42),
  [Industrial Design Act](/eng/acts/I-9), [Integrated Circuit Topography
  Act](/eng/acts/I-14.6), [Patent Act](/eng/acts/P-4), [Trademarks
  Act](/eng/acts/T-13) or any other Act of Parliament pertaining to
  intellectual or industrial property is not an anti-competitive act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made under this section in
  respect of a practice of anti-competitive acts or conduct more than
  three years after the practice or conduct has ceased.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where proceedings commenced under
  section 45, 49, 76, 90.1 or 92

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made under this section against
  a person on the basis of facts that are the same or substantially the
  same as the facts on the basis of which

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} proceedings have been commenced against that person
    under section 45 or 49; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order against that person has been made under
    section 76, 90.1 or 92.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inferences

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} In considering an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal may not draw any inference
  from the fact that the Commissioner has or has not taken any action in
  respect of the matter raised by the application.

::: HistoricalNote

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

**[[79.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-79.1 .sectionLabel}** The amount of an
administrative monetary penalty imposed on a person under subsection
79(3.1) is a debt due to Her Majesty in right of Canada and may be
recovered as such from that person in a court of competent jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 16, s. 11.5
- 2018, c. 8, s. 114(E)
:::

[Previous
Version](section-79.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 79.1"){.PITLink}

#### [Delivered Pricing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-89619 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[delivered
pricing]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[80]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-80 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of section 81, [[delivered pricing]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means the practice of refusing a customer, or a person seeking to
  become a customer, delivery of an article at any place in which the
  supplier engages in a practice of making delivery of the article to
  any other of the supplier's customers on the same trade terms that
  would be available to the first-mentioned customer if his place of
  business were located in that place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[trade
  terms]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), the expression
  [[trade terms]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means terms in respect of payment,
  units of purchase and reasonable technical and servicing requirements.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Delivered pricing

- **[[81]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-81 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  on application by the Commissioner, the Tribunal finds that delivered
  pricing is engaged in by a major supplier of an article in a market or
  is widespread in a market with the result that a customer, or a person
  seeking to become a customer, is denied an advantage that would
  otherwise be available to him in the market, the Tribunal may make an
  order prohibiting all or any of such suppliers from engaging in
  delivered pricing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception where significant capital
  investment needed

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No order shall be made against a supplier under this
  section where the Tribunal finds that the supplier could not
  accommodate any additional customers at a locality without making
  significant capital investment at that locality.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception where trademark used

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No order shall be made against a supplier under this
  section in respect of a practice of refusing a customer delivery of an
  article that the customer sells in association with a trademark that
  the supplier owns or in respect of which the supplier is a registered
  user where the Tribunal finds that the practice is necessary to
  maintain a standard of quality in respect of the article.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- [2014, c. 20, s. 366(E)](#1034903-1145665){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1034903-1145665.html"}
:::

#### [Foreign Judgments and Laws]{.HTitleText3} {#h-89636 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign judgments, etc.

**[[82]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-82 .sectionLabel}** Where, on application by
the Commissioner, the Tribunal finds that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a judgment, decree, order or other process given,
  made or issued by or out of a court or other body in a country other
  than Canada can be implemented in whole or in part by persons in
  Canada, by companies incorporated by or pursuant to an Act of
  Parliament or of the legislature of a province, or by measures taken
  in Canada, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the implementation in whole or in part of the
  judgment, decree, order or other process in Canada, would

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} adversely affect competition in Canada,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} adversely affect the efficiency of trade or
    industry in Canada without bringing about or increasing in Canada
    competition that would restore or improve that efficiency,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} adversely affect the foreign trade of Canada
    without compensating advantages, or

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} otherwise restrain or injure trade or commerce in
    Canada without compensating advantages,

  the Tribunal may, by order, direct that

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} no measures be taken in Canada to implement the
  judgment, decree, order or process, or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} no measures be taken in Canada to implement the
  judgment, decree, order or process except in such manner as the
  Tribunal prescribes for the purpose of avoiding an effect referred to
  in subparagraphs (b)(i) to (iv).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Foreign laws and directives

- **[[83]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-83 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  on application by the Commissioner, the Tribunal finds that a decision
  has been or is about to be made by a person in Canada or a company
  incorporated by or pursuant to an Act of Parliament or of the
  legislature of a province

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} as a result of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a law in force in a country other than Canada, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a directive, instruction, intimation of policy
      or other communication to that person or company or to any other
      person from

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} the government of a country other than Canada
        or of any political subdivision thereof that is in a position to
        direct or influence the policies of that person or company, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} a person in a country other than Canada who is
        in a position to direct or influence the policies of that person
        or company,

      where the communication is for the purpose of giving effect to a
      law in force in a country other than Canada,

    and that the decision, if implemented, would have or would be likely
    to have any of the effects mentioned in subparagraphs 82(b)(i) to
    (iv), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as a result of a directive, instruction, intimation
    of policy or other communication to that person or company or to any
    other person, from a person in a country other than Canada who is in
    a position to direct or influence the policies of that person or
    company, where the communication is for the purpose of giving effect
    to a conspiracy, combination, agreement or arrangement entered into
    outside Canada that, if entered into in Canada, would have been in
    contravention of section 45,

  the Tribunal may, by order, direct that

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in a case described in paragraph (a) or (b), no
    measures be taken by the person or company in Canada to implement
    the law, directive, instruction, intimation of policy or other
    communication, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in a case described in paragraph (a), no measures
    be taken by the person or company in Canada to implement the law,
    directive, instruction, intimation of policy or other communication
    except in such manner as the Tribunal prescribes for the purpose of
    avoiding an effect referred to in subparagraphs 82(b)(i) to (iv).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made by the Commissioner for an
  order under this section against a particular company where
  proceedings have been commenced under section 46 against that company
  based on the same or substantially the same facts as would be alleged
  in the application.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

#### [Foreign Suppliers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-89668 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to supply by foreign supplier

**[[84]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-84 .sectionLabel}** Where, on application by
the Commissioner, the Tribunal finds that a supplier outside Canada has
refused to supply a product or otherwise discriminated in the supply of
a product to a person in Canada (the "first" person) at the instance of
and by reason of the exertion of buying power outside Canada by another
person, the Tribunal may order any person in Canada (the "second"
person) by whom or on whose behalf or for whose benefit the buying power
was exerted

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to sell any such product of the supplier that the
  second person has obtained or obtains to the first person at the
  laid-down cost in Canada to the second person of the product and on
  the same terms and conditions as the second person obtained or obtains
  from the supplier; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} not to deal or to cease to deal, in Canada, in that
  product of the supplier.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

### [Specialization Agreements]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89675 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[85]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-85 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of this
section and sections 86 to 90,

[[article]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[article]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes each separate type, size,
    weight and quality in which an article, within the meaning assigned
    by section 2, is produced; ([article]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[registered]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[registered]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means registered in the register
    maintained pursuant to section 89; ([inscrit]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[specialization agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[specialization agreement]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an agreement
    under which each party thereto agrees to discontinue producing an
    article or service that he is engaged in producing at the time the
    agreement is entered into on the condition that each other party to
    the agreement agrees to discontinue producing an article or service
    that he is engaged in producing at the time the agreement is entered
    into, and includes any such agreement under which the parties also
    agree to buy exclusively from each other the articles or services
    that are the subject of the agreement. ([accord de
    spécialisation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order directing registration

- **[[86]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-86 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  on application by any person, and after affording the Commissioner a
  reasonable opportunity to be heard, the Tribunal finds that an
  agreement that the person who has made the application has entered
  into or is about to enter into is a specialization agreement and that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the implementation of the agreement is likely to
    bring about gains in efficiency that will be greater than, and will
    offset, the effects of any prevention or lessening of competition
    that will result or is likely to result from the agreement and the
    gains in efficiency would not likely be attained if the agreement
    were not implemented, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no attempt has been made by the persons who have
    entered or are about to enter into the agreement to coerce any
    person to become a party to the agreement,

  the Tribunal may, subject to subsection (4), make an order directing
  that the agreement be registered for a period specified in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In considering whether an agreement is likely to
  bring about gains in efficiency described in paragraph (1)(a), the
  Tribunal shall consider whether those gains will result in

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a significant increase in the real value of
    exports; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a significant substitution of domestic articles or
    services for imported articles or services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Redistribution of income does not
  result in gains in efficiency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of paragraph (1)(a), the Tribunal
  shall not find that an agreement is likely to bring about gains in
  efficiency by reason only of a redistribution of income between two or
  more persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditional orders

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where, on an application under subsection (1), the
  Tribunal finds that an agreement meets the conditions prescribed by
  paragraphs (a) and (b) of that subsection but also finds that, as a
  result of the implementation of the agreement, there is not likely to
  be substantial competition remaining in the market or markets to which
  the agreement relates, the Tribunal may provide, in an order made
  under subsection (1), that the order shall take effect only if, within
  a reasonable period of time specified in the order, there has occurred
  any of the following events, specified in the order:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the divestiture of particular assets, specified in
    the order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a wider licensing of patents, certificates of
    supplementary protection issued under the [Patent
    Act](/eng/acts/P-4) or registered integrated circuit topographies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a reduction in tariffs;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the making of an order in council under section 23
    of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11) effecting a
    remission or remissions specified in the order of the Tribunal of
    any customs duties on an article that is a subject of the agreement;
    or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the removal of import quotas or import licensing
    requirements.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1990, c. 37, s. 32
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- 2017, c. 6, s. 125
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-86-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 86"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration of modifications

- **[[87]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-87 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by the parties to a specialization agreement that has been
  registered, and after affording the Commissioner a reasonable
  opportunity to be heard, the Tribunal may make an order directing that
  a modification of the agreement be registered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to remove from register

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, on application by the Commissioner, the
  Tribunal finds that the agreement or a modification thereof that has
  been registered

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has ceased to meet the conditions prescribed by
    paragraph 86(1)(a) or (b), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is not being implemented,

  the Tribunal may make an order directing that the agreement or
  modification thereof, and any order relating thereto, be removed from
  the register.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of intervention

**[[88]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-88 .sectionLabel}** The attorney general of a
province may intervene in any proceedings before the Tribunal under
section 86 or 87 for the purpose of making representations on behalf of
the province.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Register of specialization agreements

- **[[89]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-89 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Tribunal shall cause to be maintained a register of specialization
  agreements, and any modifications of those agreements, that the
  Tribunal has directed be registered, and any such agreements and
  modifications shall be included in the register for the periods
  specified in the orders.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Public register

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The register shall be accessible to the public.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 2014, c. 20, s. 389
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-89-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 89"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of sections 45, 77 and
90.1

**[[90]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90 .sectionLabel}** Section 45, section 77 as
it applies to exclusive dealing, and section 90.1 do not apply in
respect of a specialization agreement, or any modification of such an
agreement, that is registered.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 2009, c. 2, s. 429
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-90-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 90"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Agreements or Arrangements that Prevent or Lessen Competition Substantially]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89729 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

- **[[90.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-90.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner or a person granted leave under section 103.1, the
  Tribunal finds that an agreement or arrangement or a proposed
  agreement or arrangement between persons of whom two or more are
  competitors prevents or lessens, has prevented or lessened or is
  likely to prevent or lessen competition substantially in a market, the
  Tribunal may make an order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting any person --- whether or not a party
    to the agreement or arrangement --- from doing anything under the
    agreement or arrangement; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requiring any person --- whether or not a party to
    the agreement or arrangement --- with the consent of that person and
    the Commissioner, to take any other action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception --- non competitors

  [(1.01)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal finds that a significant purpose
  of the agreement or arrangement, or any part of it, is to prevent or
  lessen competition in any market, it may make an order under
  subsection (1) even if none of the persons referred to in that
  subsection are competitors.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional or alternative order

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If, on an application under subsection (1), the
  Tribunal finds that an agreement or arrangement has had or is having
  the effect of preventing or lessening competition substantially in a
  market and that an order under subsection (1) is not likely to restore
  competition in that market, the Tribunal may, in addition to or in
  lieu of making an order under subsection (1), make an order directing
  any or all the persons against whom an order is sought to take
  actions, including the divestiture of assets or shares, that are
  reasonable and as are necessary to overcome the effects of the
  agreement or arrangement in that market.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} In making an order under subsection (1.1), the
  Tribunal shall make the order in such terms as will in its opinion
  interfere with the rights of any person to whom the order is directed
  or any other person affected by it only to the extent necessary to
  achieve the purpose of the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrative monetary penalty

  [(1.3)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal makes an order against a person
  under subsection (1) or (1.1), it may also order them to pay, in any
  manner that the Tribunal specifies, an administrative monetary penalty
  in an amount not exceeding the greater of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} \$10,000,000 and, for each subsequent order under
    either of those subsections, an amount not exceeding \$15,000,000,
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} three times the value of the benefit derived from
    the agreement or arrangement, or, if that amount cannot be
    reasonably determined, 3% of the person's annual worldwide gross
    revenues.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating or mitigating factors

  [(1.4)]{.lawlabel} In determining the amount of an administrative
  monetary penalty, the Tribunal shall take into account any evidence of
  the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the effect on competition in the relevant market;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenue from sales affected by the
    agreement or arrangement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any actual or anticipated profits affected by the
    agreement or arrangement;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the financial position of the person against whom
    the order is made;

  - [(d.1)]{.lawlabel} any amount that the person against whom the order
    is made is required to pay under an order made under subsection
    (10.1);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the history of compliance with this Act by the
    person against whom the order is made; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(1.5)]{.lawlabel} The purpose of an order made against a person under
  subsection (1.3) is to promote practices by that person that are in
  conformity with the purposes of this section and not to punish that
  person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to make the finding referred to
  in subsection (1), the Tribunal may have regard to the following
  factors:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which foreign products or foreign
    competitors provide or are likely to provide effective competition
    to the businesses of the parties to the agreement or arrangement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which acceptable substitutes for
    products supplied by the parties to the agreement or arrangement are
    or are likely to be available;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any barriers to entry into the market, including

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} tariff and non-tariff barriers to international
      trade,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} interprovincial barriers to trade, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} regulatory control over entry;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any effect of the agreement or arrangement on the
    barriers referred to in paragraph (c);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which effective competition remains
    or would remain in the market;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any removal of a vigorous and effective competitor
    that resulted from the agreement or arrangement, or any likelihood
    that the agreement or arrangement will or would result in the
    removal of such a competitor;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the nature and extent of change and innovation in
    any relevant market;

  - [(g.1)]{.lawlabel} network effects within the market;

  - [(g.2)]{.lawlabel} whether the agreement or arrangement would
    contribute to the entrenchment of the market position of leading
    incumbents;

  - [(g.3)]{.lawlabel} any effect of the agreement or arrangement on
    price or non-price competition, including quality, choice or
    consumer privacy; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that is relevant to competition in
    the market that is or would be affected by the agreement or
    arrangement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsections (1) and (2), the
  Tribunal shall not make the finding solely on the basis of evidence of
  concentration or market share.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 31, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 31, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 31, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the agreement or
  arrangement is entered into, or would be entered into, only by parties
  each of which is, in respect of every one of the others, an affiliate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the agreement or
  arrangement relates only to the export of products from Canada, unless
  the agreement or arrangement

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has resulted in or is likely to result in a
    reduction or limitation of the real value of exports of a product;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has restricted or is likely to restrict any person
    from entering into or expanding the business of exporting products
    from Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} has prevented or lessened or is likely to prevent
    or lessen competition substantially in the supply of services that
    facilitate the export of products from Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall not make an order under
  subsection (1) in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an agreement or arrangement between federal
    financial institutions, as defined in subsection 49(3), in respect
    of which the Minister of Finance has certified to the Commissioner

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the names of the parties to the agreement or
      arrangement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Minister of Finance's request for or
      approval of the agreement or arrangement for the purposes of
      financial policy;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an agreement or arrangement that constitutes a
    merger or proposed merger under the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01),
    the [Cooperative Credit Associations Act](/eng/acts/C-41.01), the
    [Insurance Companies Act](/eng/acts/I-11.8) or the [Trust and Loan
    Companies Act](/eng/acts/T-19.8) in respect of which the Minister of
    Finance has certified to the Commissioner

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the names of the parties to the agreement or
      arrangement, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the Minister of Finance's opinion that the
      merger is in the public interest, or that it would be in the
      public interest, taking into account any terms and conditions that
      may be imposed under those Acts;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an agreement or arrangement that constitutes a
    merger or proposed merger approved under subsection 53.2(7) of the
    [Canada Transportation Act](/eng/acts/C-10.4) in respect of which
    the Minister of Transport has certified to the Commissioner the
    names of the parties to the agreement or arrangement; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an agreement or arrangement that constitutes an
    existing or proposed [[arrangement]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as
    defined in section 53.7 of the [Canada Transportation
    Act](/eng/acts/C-10.4), that has been authorized by the Minister of
    Transport under subsection 53.73(8) of that Act and for which the
    authorization has not been revoked.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(9.1)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made under this section in
  respect of an agreement or arrangement that has been terminated for
  more than three years.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

  [(9.2)]{.lawlabel} The administrative monetary penalty imposed on a
  person under subsection (1.3) is a debt due to His Majesty in right of
  Canada and may be recovered as such from that person in a court of
  competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where proceedings commenced under
  section 45, 49, 76, 79 or 92

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} No application may be made under this section
  against a person on the basis of facts that are the same or
  substantially the same as the facts on the basis of which

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} proceedings have been commenced against that person
    under section 45 or 49; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order against that person has been made under
    section 76, 79 or 92.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional order --- person granted
  leave

  [(10.1)]{.lawlabel} If the Tribunal makes an order under
  subsection (1) as the result of an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, it may also order the person against whom
  the order is made to pay an amount, not exceeding the value of the
  benefit derived from the conduct that is the subject of the order, to
  be distributed among the applicant and any other person affected by
  the conduct, in any manner that the Tribunal considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation of the order

  [(10.2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may specify in an order made under
  subsection (10.1) any term that it considers necessary for the order's
  implementation, including any term referred to in any of paragraphs
  75(1.3)(a) to (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inferences

  [(10.3)]{.lawlabel} In considering an application by a person granted
  leave under section 103.1, the Tribunal may not draw any inference
  from the fact that the Commissioner has or has not taken any action in
  respect of the matter raised by the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
  [[competitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} In subsections (1) and (1.01),
  [[competitor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a person who it is
  reasonable to believe would be likely to compete with respect to a
  product in the absence of the agreement or arrangement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2009, c. 2, s. 429
- 2018, c. 8, s. 115
- [2018, c. 10, s. 87](#1037875-1122488){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037875-1122488.html"}
- [2022, c. 10, s. 263](#1357352-1366473){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357352-1366473.html"}
- [2023, c. 31, s. 8](#1431674-1501266){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1431674-1501266.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 248](#1462856-1484100){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462856-1484100.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 271](#1462964-1501267){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462964-1501267.html"}
:::

### [Mergers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89782 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of
[[merger]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

**[[91]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-91 .sectionLabel}** In sections 92 to 100,
[[merger]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the acquisition or establishment,
direct or indirect, by one or more persons, whether by purchase or lease
of shares or assets, by amalgamation or by combination or otherwise, of
control over or significant interest in the whole or a part of a
business of a competitor, supplier, customer or other person.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order

- **[[92]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-92 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where,
  on application by the Commissioner, the Tribunal finds that a merger
  or proposed merger prevents or lessens, or is likely to prevent or
  lessen, competition substantially

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in a trade, industry or profession,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} among the sources from which a trade, industry or
    profession obtains a product, including labour,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} among the outlets through which a trade, industry
    or profession disposes of a product, including labour, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} otherwise than as described in paragraphs (a) to
    (c),

  the Tribunal may, subject to sections 94 and 95,

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a completed merger, in order to
    restore competition to the level that would have prevailed but for
    the merger, order any party to the merger or any other person

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to dissolve the merger in such manner as the
      Tribunal directs,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to dispose of assets or shares designated by the
      Tribunal in such manner as the Tribunal directs, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to or in lieu of the action
      referred to in subparagraph (i) or (ii), with the consent of the
      person against whom the order is directed and the Commissioner, to
      take any other action, or

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a proposed merger, in order to
    preserve the level of competition that would prevail but for the
    merger, make an order directed against any party to the proposed
    merger or any other person

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} ordering the person against whom the order is
      directed not to proceed with the merger,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} ordering the person against whom the order is
      directed not to proceed with a part of the merger, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} in addition to or in lieu of the order referred
      to in subparagraph (ii), either or both

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} prohibiting the person against whom the order
        is directed, should the merger or part thereof be completed,
        from doing any act or thing the prohibition of which the
        Tribunal determines to be necessary to ensure that the merger or
        part thereof does not prevent or lessen competition, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} with the consent of the person against whom the
        order is directed and the Commissioner, ordering the person to
        take any other action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of this section, if the Tribunal
  finds, on a balance of probabilities, that a merger or proposed merger
  results or is likely to result in a significant increase in
  concentration or market share, the Tribunal shall also find that the
  merger or proposed merger prevents or lessens, or is likely to prevent
  or lessen, competition substantially, unless the contrary is proved on
  a balance of probabilities by the parties to the merger or proposed
  merger.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Significant increase in concentration
  or market share

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A merger or proposed merger results or is likely to
  result in a significant increase in concentration or market share if,
  in any relevant market, as a result of the merger or proposed merger,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentration index increases or is likely to
    increase by more than 100; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} either

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the concentration index is or is likely to be
      more than 1,800, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the market share of the parties to the merger or
      proposed merger is or is likely to be more than 30%.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[concentration
  index]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (3), [[concentration
  index]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in any relevant market, the sum of
  the squares of the market shares of the suppliers or customers.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations --- different values

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may by regulation prescribe
  different values than those provided in subsection (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- [2023, c. 31, s. 9](#1431680-1432234){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1431680-1432234.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 249](#1462871-1484125){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462871-1484125.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-92-20231215.html "Link to previous version of section 92"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered regarding
prevention or lessening of competition

**[[93]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-93 .sectionLabel}** In determining, for the
purpose of section 92, whether or not a merger or proposed merger
prevents or lessens, or is likely to prevent or lessen, competition
substantially, the Tribunal may have regard to the following factors:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which foreign products or foreign
  competitors provide or are likely to provide effective competition to
  the businesses of the parties to the merger or proposed merger;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether the business, or a part of the business, of a
  party to the merger or proposed merger has failed or is likely to
  fail;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which acceptable substitutes for
  products supplied by the parties to the merger or proposed merger are
  or are likely to be available;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} any barriers to entry into a market, including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} tariff and non-tariff barriers to international
    trade,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} interprovincial barriers to trade, and

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} regulatory control over entry,

  and any effect of the merger or proposed merger on such barriers;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which effective competition remains or
  would remain in a market that is or would be affected by the merger or
  proposed merger;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} any likelihood that the merger or proposed merger
  will or would result in the removal of a vigorous and effective
  competitor;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} the nature and extent of change and innovation in a
  relevant market;

- [(g.1)]{.lawlabel} network effects within a market;

- [(g.2)]{.lawlabel} whether the merger or proposed merger would
  contribute to the entrenchment of the market position of leading
  incumbents;

- [(g.3)]{.lawlabel} any effect of the merger or proposed merger on
  price or non-price competition, including quality, choice or consumer
  privacy;

- [(g.4)]{.lawlabel} the change in concentration or market share that
  the merger or proposed merger has brought about or is likely to bring
  about;

- [(g.5)]{.lawlabel} any likelihood that the merger or proposed merger
  will or would result in express or tacit coordination between
  competitors in a market; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} any other factor that is relevant to competition in a
  market that is or would be affected by the merger or proposed merger.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- [2022, c. 10, s. 264](#1357354-1366478){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357354-1366478.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 250](#1462882-1484147){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462882-1484147.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-93-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 93"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[94]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-94 .sectionLabel}** The Tribunal shall not
make an order under section 92 in respect of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a merger substantially completed before the coming
  into force of this section;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a merger or proposed merger under the [Bank
  Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), the [Cooperative Credit Associations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-41.01), the [Insurance Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/I-11.8) or the [Trust and Loan Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/T-19.8) in respect of which the Minister of Finance has
  certified to the Commissioner the names of the parties and that the
  merger is in the public interest --- or that it would be in the public
  interest, taking into account any terms and conditions that may be
  imposed under those Acts;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a merger or proposed merger approved under subsection
  53.2(7) of the [Canada Transportation Act](/eng/acts/C-10.4) and in
  respect of which the Minister of Transport has certified to the
  Commissioner the names of the parties; or

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} a merger or proposed merger that constitutes an
  existing or proposed [[arrangement]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined
  in section 53.7 of the [Canada Transportation Act](/eng/acts/C-10.4),
  that has been authorized by the Minister of Transport under subsection
  53.73(8) of that Act and for which the authorization has not been
  revoked.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1991, c. 45, s. 549, c. 46, ss. 592, 593, c. 47, s. 716
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- 2000, c. 15, s. 14
- 2001, c. 9, s. 579
- 2007, c. 19, s. 62
- [2018, c. 10, s. 88](#1037877-1122492){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1037877-1122492.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-94-20070622.html "Link to previous version of section 94"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception for joint ventures

- **[[95]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-95 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Tribunal shall not make an order under section 92 in respect of a
  combination formed or proposed to be formed, otherwise than through a
  corporation, to undertake a specific project or a program of research
  and development if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a project or program of that nature

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} would not have taken place or be likely to take
      place in the absence of the combination, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} would not reasonably have taken place or
      reasonably be likely to take place in the absence of the
      combination because of the risks involved in relation to the
      project or program and the business to which it relates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no change in control over any party to the
    combination resulted or would result from the combination;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all the persons who formed the combination are
    parties to an agreement in writing that imposes on one or more of
    them an obligation to contribute assets and governs a continuing
    relationship between those parties;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the agreement referred to in paragraph (c)
    restricts the range of activities that may be carried on pursuant to
    the combination, and provides that the agreement terminates on the
    completion of the project or program; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the combination does not prevent or lessen or is
    not likely to prevent or lessen competition except to the extent
    reasonably required to undertake and complete the project or
    program.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, this section does not apply in
  respect of the acquisition of assets of a combination.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

**[[96]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-96 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 31,
s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

**[[97]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-97 .sectionLabel}** No application may be
made under section 92,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a merger that was the subject of a
  request for a certificate under section 102 or a notification under
  section 114, more than one year after the merger has been
  substantially completed; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of any other merger, more than three years
  after the merger has been substantially completed.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 2009, c. 2, s. 430

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where proceedings commenced under section
45, 49, 79 or 90.1

**[[98]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-98 .sectionLabel}** No application may be
made under section 92 against a person on the basis of facts that are
the same or substantially the same as the facts on the basis of which

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} proceedings have been commenced against that person
  under section 45 or 49; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order against that person has been made under
  section 79 or 90.1.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 2009, c. 2, s. 430
- [2024, c. 15, s. 252](#1462889-1484154){.wb-lbx

:::

Version](section-98-20090312.html "Link to previous version of section 98"){.PITLink}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditional orders directing dissolution
of a merger

- **[[99]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-99 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Tribunal may provide, in an order made under section 92 directing a
  person to dissolve a merger or to dispose of assets or shares, that
  the order may be rescinded or varied if, within a reasonable period of
  time specified in the order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there has occurred

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a reduction, removal or remission, specified in
      the order, of any relevant customs duties, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a reduction or removal, specified in the order,
      of prohibitions, controls or regulations imposed by or pursuant to
      any Act of Parliament on the importation into Canada of an article
      specified in the order, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that person or any other person has taken any
    action specified in the order

  that will, in the opinion of the Tribunal, prevent the merger from
  preventing or lessening competition substantially.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When conditional order may be rescinded
  or varied

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where, on application by any person against whom an
  order under section 92 is directed, the Tribunal is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reduction, removal or remission specified in the
    order pursuant to paragraph (1)(a) has occurred, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the action specified in the order pursuant to
    paragraph (1)(b) has been taken,

  the Tribunal may rescind or vary the order accordingly.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order where no application under
section 92

- **[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Tribunal may issue an interim order forbidding any person named in the
  application from doing any act or thing that it appears to the
  Tribunal may constitute or be directed toward the completion or
  implementation of a proposed merger in respect of which an application
  has not been made under section 92 or previously under this section,
  where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on application by the Commissioner, certifying that
    an inquiry is being made under paragraph 10(1)(b) and that, in the
    Commissioner's opinion, more time is required to complete the
    inquiry, the Tribunal finds that in the absence of an interim order
    a party to the proposed merger or any other person is likely to take
    an action that would substantially impair the ability of the
    Tribunal to remedy the effect of the proposed merger on competition
    under that section because that action would be difficult to
    reverse; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Tribunal finds, on application by the
    Commissioner, that the completion of the proposed merger would
    result in a contravention of section 114.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), at least forty-eight hours
  notice of an application for an interim order under subsection (1)
  shall be given by or on behalf of the Commissioner to each person
  against whom the order is sought.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
  application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Tribunal is satisfied, in respect of an
  application for an interim order under paragraph (1)(b), that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subsection (2) cannot reasonably be complied with,
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the urgency of the situation is such that service
    of notice in accordance with subsection (2) would not be in the
    public interest,

  it may proceed with the application [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of application for interim order

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If an application for an interim order is made
  under subsection (1) in respect of a proposed merger, the merger shall
  not be completed until the application has been disposed of by the
  Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of interim order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An interim order issued under subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall be on such terms as the Tribunal considers
    necessary and sufficient to meet the circumstances of the case; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsections (5) and (6), shall have
    effect for such period of time as is specified in it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order: inquiry

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The duration of an interim order issued under
  paragraph (1)(a) shall not exceed thirty days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of order: failure to comply

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The duration of an interim order issued under
  paragraph (1)(b) shall not exceed

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} ten days after section 114 is complied with, in the
    case of an interim order issued on [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"}
    application; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} thirty days after section 114 is complied with, in
    any other case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where the Tribunal finds, on application made by the
  Commissioner on forty-eight hours notice to each person to whom an
  interim order is directed, that the Commissioner is unable to complete
  an inquiry within the period specified in the order because of
  circumstances beyond the control of the Commissioner, the Tribunal may
  extend the duration of the order to a day not more than sixty days
  after the order takes effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Completion of inquiry

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Where an interim order is issued under paragraph
  (1)(a), the Commissioner shall proceed as expeditiously as possible to
  complete the inquiry under section 10 in respect of the proposed
  merger.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, ss. 24, 37

  ajax-after="1357356-1366480.html"}

  ajax-after="1462891-1483902.html"}
:::
