[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of intervention

**[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101 .sectionLabel}** The attorney general of
a province may intervene in any proceedings before the Tribunal under
section 92 for the purpose of making representations on behalf of the
province.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Advance ruling certificates

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Commissioner is satisfied
  by a party or parties to a proposed transaction that he would not have
  sufficient grounds on which to apply to the Tribunal under section 92,
  the Commissioner may issue a certificate to the effect that he is so
  satisfied.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of Commissioner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall consider any request for a
  certificate under this section as expeditiously as possible.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No application under section 92

**[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** Where the Commissioner
issues a certificate under section 102, the Commissioner shall not, if
the transaction to which the certificate relates is substantially
completed within one year after the certificate is issued, apply to the
Tribunal under section 92 in respect of the transaction solely on the
basis of information that is the same or substantially the same as the
information on the basis of which the certificate was issued.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
:::

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-89925 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Leave to make application under section
74.1, 75, 76, 77, 79 or 90.1

- **[[103.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any person may apply to the Tribunal
  for leave to make an application under section 74.1, 75, 76, 77, 79 or
  90.1. The application for leave must be accompanied by an affidavit
  setting out the facts in support of the person's application under
  that section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The applicant must serve a copy of the application
  for leave on the Commissioner and any person against whom the order is
  sought under section 74.1, 75, 76, 77, 79 or 90.1, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certification by Commissioner

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall, within 48 hours after
  receiving a copy of an application for leave, certify to the Tribunal
  whether or not the matter in respect of which leave is sought

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is the subject of an inquiry by the Commissioner;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was the subject of an inquiry that has been
    discontinued because of a settlement between the Commissioner and
    the person against whom the order is sought under section 74.1, 75,
    76, 77, 79 or 90.1, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application discontinued

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal is not to consider an application for
  leave respecting a matter described in paragraph (3)(a) or (b) or a
  matter that is the subject of an application already submitted to the
  Tribunal by the Commissioner under section 74.1, 75, 76, 77, 79 or
  90.1.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice by Tribunal

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal shall as soon as practicable after
  receiving the Commissioner's certification under subsection (3) notify
  the applicant and any person against whom the order is sought as to
  whether it can hear the application for leave.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A person served with an application for leave may,
  within 15 days after receiving notice under subsection (5), make
  representations in writing to the Tribunal and shall serve a copy of
  the representations on any other person referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting leave --- section 74.1

  [(6.1)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may grant leave to make an application
  under section 74.1 if it is satisfied that it is in the public
  interest to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting leave --- sections 75, 77, 79
  or 90.1

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may grant leave to make an application
  under section 75, 77, 79 or 90.1 if it has reason to believe that the
  applicant is directly and substantially affected in the whole or part
  of the applicant's business by any conduct referred to in one of those
  sections that could be subject to an order under that section or if it
  is satisfied that it is in the public interest to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting leave to make application
  under section 76

  [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may grant leave to make an application
  under section 76 if it has reason to believe that the applicant is
  directly affected by any conduct referred to in that section that
  could be subject to an order under that section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Granting leave --- section 90.1

  [(7.2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal is not to consider an application for
  leave in respect of an application under section 90.1 that relates to
  an agreement or arrangement for which a certificate issued under
  subsection 124.3(1) is valid and registered.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time and conditions for making
  application

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may set the time within which and the
  conditions subject to which an application under section 74.1, 75, 76,
  77, 79 or 90.1 must be made. The application must be made no more than
  one year after the practice or conduct that is the subject of the
  application has ceased.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Decision

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal must give written reasons for its
  decision to grant or refuse leave and send copies to the applicant,
  the Commissioner and any other person referred to in subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may not make an application for an
  order under section 74.1, 75, 76, 77, 79 or 90.1 on the basis of the
  same or substantially the same facts as are alleged in a matter for
  which the Tribunal has granted leave under subsection (6.1), (7) or
  (7.1), if the person granted leave has already applied to the Tribunal
  under one of those sections.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inferences

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} In considering an application for leave, the
  Tribunal may not draw any inference from the fact that the
  Commissioner has or has not taken any action in respect of the matter
  raised by it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inquiry by Commissioner

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} If the Commissioner has certified under
  subsection (3) that a matter in respect of which leave was sought by a
  person is under inquiry and the Commissioner subsequently discontinues
  the inquiry other than by way of settlement, the Commissioner shall,
  as soon as practicable, notify that person that the inquiry is
  discontinued.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 16, s. 12
- 2009, c. 2, s. 431
- [2022, c. 10, s. 266](#1357358-1366494){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357358-1366494.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 254](#1462893-1532052){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462893-1532052.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-103.1-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 103.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention by Commissioner

**[[103.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103.2 .sectionLabel}** If a person granted
leave under subsection 103.1(6.1), (7) or (7.1) makes an application
under section 74.1, 75, 76, 77, 79 or 90.1, the Commissioner may
intervene in the proceedings.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 16, s. 12
- 2009, c. 2, s. 432
- [2022, c. 10, s. 267](#1357371-1366497){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357371-1366497.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 255](#1462908-1532055){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462908-1532055.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-103.2-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 103.2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order

- **[[103.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  Tribunal may, on [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application by the
  Commissioner in which the Commissioner certifies that an inquiry is
  being made under paragraph 10(1)(b), issue an interim order

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to prevent the continuation of conduct that could
    be the subject of an order under any of sections 75 to 77, 79, 81,
    84 or 90.1; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to prevent the taking of measures under section 82
    or 83.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may make the interim order if it finds
  that the conduct or measures could be of the type described in
  paragraph (1)(a) or (b) and that, in the absence of an interim order,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} injury to competition that cannot adequately be
    remedied by the Tribunal is likely to occur;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person is likely to be eliminated as a
    competitor; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person is likely to suffer a significant loss of
    market share, a significant loss of revenue or other harm that
    cannot be adequately remedied by the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before making an application for an order to prevent
  the continuation of conduct that could be the subject of an order
  under any of sections 75 to 77, 79, 81, 84 or 90.1 by an entity
  incorporated under the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01), the [Insurance
  Companies Act](/eng/acts/I-11.8), the [Trust and Loan Companies
  Act](/eng/acts/T-19.8) or the [Cooperative Credit Associations
  Act](/eng/acts/C-41.01) or a subsidiary of such an entity, the
  Commissioner must consult with the Minister of Finance respecting the
  safety and soundness of the entity.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (5) and (6), an interim order
  has effect for 10 days, beginning on the day on which it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension or revocation of order

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may, on application by the Commissioner
  on 48 hours notice to each person against whom the interim order is
  directed,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} extend the interim order once or twice for
    additional periods of 35 days each; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} rescind the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to Tribunal for extension

  [(5.1)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner may, before the expiry of the
  second 35-day period referred to in subsection (5) or of the period
  fixed by the Tribunal under subsection (7), as the case may be, apply
  to the Tribunal for a further extension of the interim order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of application by Commissioner

  [(5.2)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner shall give at least 48 hours
  notice of an application referred to in subsection (5.1) to the person
  against whom the interim order is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of interim order

  [(5.3)]{.lawlabel} The Tribunal may order that the effective period of
  the interim order be extended if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Commissioner establishes that information
    requested for the purpose of the inquiry has not yet been provided
    or that more time is needed in order to review the information;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information was requested during the initial
    period that the interim order had effect, within the first 35 days
    after an order extending the interim order under subsection (5) had
    effect, or within the first 35 days after an order extending the
    interim order made under subsection (7) had effect, as the case may
    be, and

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the provision of such information is the subject
      of a written undertaking, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the information was ordered to be provided under
      section 11; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the information is reasonably required to determine
    whether grounds exist for the Commissioner to make an application
    under any section referred to in paragraph (1)(a) or (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms

  [(5.4)]{.lawlabel} An order extending an interim order issued under
  subsection (5.3) shall have effect for such period as the Tribunal
  considers necessary to give the Commissioner a reasonable opportunity
  to receive and review the information referred to in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of application

  [(5.5)]{.lawlabel} If an application is made under subsection (5.1),
  the interim order has effect until the Tribunal makes a decision
  whether to grant an extension under subsection (5.3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When application made to Tribunal

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} If an application is made under subsection (7), an
  interim order has effect until the Tribunal makes an order under that
  subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confirming or setting aside interim
  order

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A person against whom the Tribunal has made an
  interim order may apply to the Tribunal in the first 10 days during
  which the order has effect to have it varied or set aside and the
  Tribunal shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if it is satisfied that one or more of the
    situations set out in paragraphs (2)(a) to (c) existed or are likely
    to exist, make an order confirming the interim order, with or
    without variation as the Tribunal considers necessary and sufficient
    to meet the circumstances, and fix the effective period of that
    order for a maximum of 70 days, beginning on the day on which the
    order confirming the interim order is made; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if it is not satisfied that any of the situations
    set out in paragraphs (2)(a) to (c) existed or is likely to exist,
    make an order setting aside the interim order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A person who makes an application under
  subsection (7) shall give the Commissioner 48 hours written notice of
  the application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Representations

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} At the hearing of an application under subsection
  (7), the Tribunal shall provide the applicant, the Commissioner and
  any person directly affected by the interim order with a full
  opportunity to present evidence and make representations before the
  Tribunal makes an order under that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition of extraordinary relief

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Notwithstanding section 13 of the [Competition
  Tribunal Act](/eng/acts/C-36.4), an interim order shall not be
  appealed or reviewed in any court except as provided for by subsection
  (7).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of Commissioner

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} When an interim order is in effect, the Commissioner
  shall proceed as expeditiously as possible to complete the inquiry
  arising out of the conduct in respect of which the order was made.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 16, s. 12
- 2017, c. 26, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-103.3-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 103.3"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order

- **[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an application has been made for an order under this Part, other than
  an interim order under section 100 or 103.3, the Tribunal, on
  application by the Commissioner or a person who has made an
  application under section 75, 76, 77, 79 or 90.1, may issue any
  interim order that it considers appropriate, having regard to the
  principles ordinarily considered by superior courts when granting
  interlocutory or injunctive relief.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of application for interim order

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} If an application for an interim order is made
  under subsection (1) in respect of a proposed merger, the merger shall
  not be completed until the application has been disposed of by the
  Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of interim order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An interim order issued under subsection (1) shall be
  on such terms, and shall have effect for such period of time, as the
  Tribunal considers necessary and sufficient to meet the circumstances
  of the case.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of Commissioner

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where an interim order issued under subsection (1) on
  application by the Commissioner is in effect, the Commissioner shall
  proceed as expeditiously as possible to complete proceedings under
  this Part arising out of the conduct in respect of which the order was
  issued.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- 2002, c. 16, s. 13
- 2015, c. 3, s. 39
- [2022, c. 10, s. 268](#1357373-1366499){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357373-1366499.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 256](#1462910-1483905){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462910-1483905.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-104-20240620.html "Link to previous version of section 104"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[104.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2009,
c. 2, s. 433\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-104.1-20050401.html "Link to previous version of section 104.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent agreement

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Commissioner and a person in respect of whom the Commissioner has
  applied or may apply for an order under this Part, other than an
  interim order under section 103.3, may sign a consent agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Terms of consent agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement shall be based on terms that
  could be the subject of an order of the Tribunal against that person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement may be filed with the Tribunal
  for immediate registration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of registration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Upon registration of the consent agreement, the
  proceedings, if any, are terminated, and the consent agreement has the
  same force and effect, and proceedings may be taken, as if it were an
  order of the Tribunal.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- 2002, c. 16, s. 14
- 2009, c. 2, s. 434
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-105-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 105"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Rescission or variation of consent
agreement or order

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Tribunal may rescind or vary a consent agreement or an order made
  under this Part other than an order under section 103.3 or a consent
  agreement under section 106.1, on application by the Commissioner or
  the person who consented to the agreement, or the person against whom
  the order was made, if the Tribunal finds that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances that led to the making of the
    agreement or order have changed and, in the circumstances that exist
    at the time the application is made, the agreement or order would
    not have been made or would have been ineffective in achieving its
    intended purpose; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Commissioner and the person who consented to
    the agreement have consented to an alternative agreement or the
    Commissioner and the person against whom the order was made have
    consented to an alternative order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Directly affected persons

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person directly affected by a consent agreement,
  other than a party to that agreement, may apply to the Tribunal within
  60 days after the registration of the agreement to have one or more of
  its terms rescinded or varied. The Tribunal may grant the application
  if it finds that the person has established that the terms could not
  be the subject of an order of the Tribunal.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 37
- 2002, c. 16, s. 14
- 2009, c. 2, s. 435
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-106-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 106"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consent agreement --- parties to a
private action

- **[[106.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a person granted leave under
  section 103.1 makes an application to the Tribunal for an order under
  section 75, 76, 77, 79 or 90.1 and the terms of the order are agreed
  to by the person in respect of whom the order is sought and consistent
  with the provisions of this Act, a consent agreement may be filed with
  the Tribunal for registration.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Commissioner

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On filing the consent agreement with the Tribunal for
  registration, the parties shall serve a copy of it on the Commissioner
  without delay.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement shall be published without
  delay in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The consent agreement shall be registered 30 days
  after its publication unless a third party makes an application to the
  Tribunal before then to cancel the agreement or replace it with an
  order of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of registration

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Upon registration, the consent agreement has the same
  force and effect, and proceedings may be taken, as if it were an order
  of the Tribunal.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner may intervene

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Commissioner, the Tribunal may
  vary or rescind a registered consent agreement if it finds that the
  agreement has or is likely to have anti-competitive effects.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner must give notice of an application
  under subsection (6) to the parties to the consent agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2002, c. 16, s. 14
- 2015, c. 3, s. 40
- [2022, c. 10, s. 269](#1357375-1366501){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357375-1366501.html"}
- [2024, c. 15, s. 257](#1462915-1532058){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462915-1532058.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-106.1-20220623.html "Link to previous version of section 106.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to comply with consent agreement

- **[[106.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner, the Tribunal determines that a person, without good and
  sufficient cause, the proof of which lies on the person, has failed to
  comply or is likely to fail to comply with a consent agreement
  registered under subsection 105(3) or 106.1(4), the Tribunal may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prohibit the person from doing anything that, in
    the Tribunal's opinion, may constitute a failure to comply with the
    agreement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order the person to take any action that is
    necessary to comply with the agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order the person to pay, in any manner that the
    Tribunal specifies, an administrative monetary penalty in an amount
    not exceeding \$10,000 for each day on which they fail to comply
    with the agreement, determined by the Tribunal after taking into
    account any evidence of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person's financial position,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's history of compliance with this
      Act,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the period of non-compliance,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} grant any other relief that the Tribunal considers
    appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The terms of an order under paragraph (1)(c) are to
  be determined with a view to promoting conduct by the person that is
  in conformity with the purposes of this Act and not with a view to
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrative monetary penalty imposed under
  paragraph (1)(c) is a debt due to His Majesty in right of Canada and
  may be recovered as such from the person in a court of competent
  jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 258](#1462917-1483922){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462917-1483922.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of agreement on Commissioner

- **[[106.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106.3
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a person granted leave under
  section 103.1 makes an application to the Tribunal for an order under
  section 75, 76, 77, 79 or 90.1 and the person discontinues the
  application by reason of having entered into an agreement with any
  other person, the parties to the agreement must serve a copy of it on
  the Commissioner within 10 days after the day on which it was entered
  into.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Commissioner may intervene

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On application by the Commissioner, the Tribunal may
  vary or rescind the agreement if it finds that the agreement has or is
  likely to have anti-competitive effects.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Commissioner must give notice of an application
  under subsection (2) to the parties to the agreement.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 259](#1462919-1532067){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462919-1532067.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Failure to serve

- **[[106.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If, on application by the
  Commissioner, the Tribunal determines that a person, without good and
  sufficient cause, the proof of which lies on the person, has failed to
  serve a copy of an agreement on the Commissioner in accordance with
  subsection 106.3(1), the Tribunal may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order the person to serve the Commissioner with a
    copy of the agreement;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} issue an interim order prohibiting any person from
    doing anything that, in the Tribunal's opinion, may constitute or be
    directed toward the implementation of the agreement;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} order the person to pay, in any manner that the
    Tribunal specifies, an administrative monetary penalty in an amount
    not exceeding \$10,000 for each day on which they fail to serve a
    copy of the agreement on the Commissioner, determined by the
    Tribunal after taking into account any evidence of the following:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the person's financial position,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the person's history of compliance with this
      Act,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the duration of the period of non-compliance,
      and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} grant any other relief that the Tribunal considers
    appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The terms of an order under paragraph (1)(c) are to
  be determined with a view to promoting conduct by the person that is
  in conformity with the purposes of this Act and not with a view to
  punishment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The administrative monetary penalty imposed under
  paragraph (1)(c) is a debt due to His Majesty in right of Canada and
  may be recovered as such from the person in a court of competent
  jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 259](#1462919-1532084){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462919-1532084.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

**[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107 .sectionLabel}** In determining whether
or not to make an order under this Part, the Tribunal shall not exclude
from consideration any evidence by reason only that it might be evidence
in respect of an offence under this Act or in respect of which another
order could be made by the Tribunal under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

## [PART VIII.1]{.HLabel1}[Matters Reviewable by a Court]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1483923 .Part}

### [Definitions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1483924 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[107.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107.1 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Part:

[[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[court]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Federal Court or the
    superior court of a province. ([tribunal]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[reprisal action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[reprisal action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an action taken by a
    person to penalize, punish, discipline, harass or disadvantage
    another person because of that person's communications with the
    Commissioner or because that person has cooperated, testified or
    assisted, or has expressed an intention to cooperate, testify or
    assist in an investigation or proceeding under this
    Act. ([représailles]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 260](#1462921-1483930){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462921-1483930.html"}
:::

### [Reprisal Action]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1483931 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition orders

**[[107.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107.2 .sectionLabel}** If, following an
application by the Commissioner or a person directly and substantially
affected by an alleged reprisal action, a court concludes that a person
is engaging, has engaged or is likely to engage in a reprisal action, it
may make an order prohibiting the person from engaging in that action.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 260](#1462921-1483935){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462921-1483935.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Administrative Monetary Penalties

**[[107.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107.3 .sectionLabel}** If the court makes
an order against a person under section 107.2 on the basis that the
person is engaging in or has engaged in a reprisal action, it may also
order them to pay an administrative monetary penalty, in any manner that
the court specifies, in an amount not exceeding

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an individual, \$750,000 and for each
  subsequent order, \$1,000,000; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a corporation, \$10,000,000 and for
  each subsequent order, \$15,000,000.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 260](#1462921-1483941){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462921-1483941.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of order

**[[107.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107.4 .sectionLabel}** The terms of an
order made against a person under section 107.3 are to be determined
with a view to promoting conduct by that person that is in conformity
with the purposes of this Act and not with a view to punishment.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 260](#1462921-1483945){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462921-1483945.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aggravating or mitigating factors

**[[107.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107.5 .sectionLabel}** Any evidence of the
following shall be taken into account in determining the amount of an
administrative monetary penalty under section 107.3:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the frequency and duration of the conduct;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vulnerability of the class of persons likely to
  be adversely affected by the conduct;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the financial position of the person against whom the
  order is made;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the history of compliance with this Act by the person
  against whom the order is made; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant factor.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 260](#1462921-1483954){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462921-1483954.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Unpaid monetary penalty

**[[107.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107.6 .sectionLabel}** The administrative
monetary penalty imposed under section 107.3 is a debt due to His
Majesty in right of Canada and may be recovered as such from that person
in a court of competent jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2024, c. 15, s. 260](#1462921-1483958){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462921-1483958.html"}
:::

## [PART IX]{.HLabel1}[Notifiable Transactions]{.HTitleText1} {#h-90061 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-90062 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this Part,

  [[equity interest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[equity interest]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a corporation, a share in the
        corporation; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an entity other than a
        corporation, an interest that entitles the holder of that
        interest to receive profits of that entity or assets of that
        entity on its dissolution; ([intérêt relatif à des capitaux
        propres]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[operating business]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[operating business]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a business
      undertaking in Canada to which employees employed in connection
      with the undertaking ordinarily report for work; ([entreprise en
      exploitation]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an entity, an individual, a
      trustee, an executor, an administrator or a liquidator of the
      succession, an administrator of the property of others or a
      representative, but does not include a bare trustee or a trustee
      responsible exclusively for preserving and transferring the
      property of a person; ([personne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribed by regulations
      made under section 124; ([réglementaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[voting share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[voting share]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any share that carries
      voting rights under all circumstances or by reason of an event
      that has occurred and is continuing. ([actions comportant droit de
      vote]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entities controlled by Her Majesty

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part, except section 113,
  one entity is not affiliated with another entity by reason only of the
  fact that both entities are controlled by Her Majesty in right of
  Canada or a province, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Computation of time

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In this Part, a time period is calculated in
  accordance with sections 26 to 30 of the [Interpretation
  Act](/eng/acts/I-21) except that the following days are also
  considered to be a [[holiday]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in
  subsection 35(1) of that Act:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} Saturday;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if Christmas Day falls on a Saturday or Sunday, the
    following Monday and Tuesday; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if another holiday falls on a Saturday or Sunday,
    the following Monday.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission after 5:00 p.m.

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part, anything submitted to
  the Commissioner after 5:00 p.m. (Eastern Time) is deemed to be
  received by the Commissioner on the next day that is not a holiday.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 25
- 2018, c. 8, s. 116
- [2022, c. 10, s. 270](#1357377-1366509){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1357377-1366509.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-108-20180501.html "Link to previous version of section 108"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-90077 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General limit relating to parties

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part does not apply in respect of a proposed transaction unless the
  parties thereto, together with their affiliates,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} have assets in Canada that exceed four hundred
    million dollars in aggregate value, determined as of such time and
    in such manner as may be prescribed, or such greater amount as may
    be prescribed; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} had gross revenues from sales in, from or into
    Canada, determined for such annual period and in such manner as may
    be prescribed, that exceed four hundred million dollars in aggregate
    value, or such greater amount as may be prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Parties to acquisition of shares or
  interest

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Part,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the parties to a proposed acquisition of shares are
    the person or persons who propose to acquire the shares and the
    corporation whose shares are to be acquired; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the parties to a proposed acquisition of an
    interest in a combination are the person or persons who propose to
    acquire the interest and the combination whose interest is to be
    acquired.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 26
- 2018, c. 8, s. 117
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-109-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 109"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of Part

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies only in respect of proposed transactions described in
  this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquisition of assets

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 111 and 113, this Part applies in
  respect of a proposed acquisition of any of the assets in Canada and,
  if any, outside Canada, of an operating business if the aggregate
  value of the assets in Canada, determined as of the time and in the
  manner that is prescribed, or the gross revenues from sales in, from
  or into Canada generated from all the assets proposed to be acquired,
  determined for the annual period and in the manner that is prescribed,
  would exceed the amount determined under subsection (7) or (8), as the
  case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquisition of shares

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 111 and 113, this Part applies in
  respect of a proposed acquisition of voting shares of a corporation
  that carries on an operating business or controls an entity that
  carries on an operating business

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate value of the assets in Canada,
      determined as of the time and in the manner that is prescribed,
      that are owned by the corporation or by entities controlled by
      that corporation, other than assets that are equity interests in
      those entities, would exceed the amount set out in subsection (7)
      or the amount determined under subsection (8), as the case may be,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenues from sales in, from or into
      Canada, determined for the annual period and in the manner that is
      prescribed, generated from all the assets that are owned by the
      corporation or by entities controlled by that corporation would
      exceed the amount determined under subsection (7) or (8), as the
      case may be; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if, as a result of the proposed acquisition of the
    voting shares, the person or persons acquiring the shares, together
    with their affiliates, would own voting shares of the corporation
    that in the aggregate carry more than the following percentages of
    the votes attached to all the corporation's outstanding voting
    shares:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} 20%, if any of the corporation's voting shares
      are publicly traded,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} 35%, if none of the corporation's voting shares
      are publicly traded, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} 50%, if the person or persons already own more
      than the percentage set out in subparagraph (i) or (ii), as the
      case may be, before the proposed acquisition.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquisition of assets and shares

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} If a proposed transaction would be completed
  through an acquisition of assets referred to in subsection (2) and
  shares referred to in subsection (3),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the value of the assets calculated under
    subsection (2) and the value of the assets calculated under
    subparagraph (3)(a)(i) are to be aggregated for the purpose of
    determining if those assets exceed in aggregate value the amount
    determined under subsection (8); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenues calculated under subsection (2)
    and the gross revenues calculated under subparagraph (3)(a)(ii) are
    to be aggregated for the purpose of determining if those gross
    revenues exceed in aggregate value the amount determined under
    subsection (8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amalgamation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4.1) and section 113, this
  Part applies in respect of a proposed amalgamation of two or more
  entities if one or more of those entities carries on an operating
  business, or controls an entity that carries on an operating business,
  and if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate value of the assets in Canada,
    determined as of the time and in the manner that is prescribed, that
    would be owned by the continuing entity that would result from the
    amalgamation or by entities controlled by the continuing entity,
    other than assets that are equity interests in those entities, would
    exceed the amount set out in subsection (7) or the amount determined
    under subsection (8), as the case may be; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenues from sales in, from or into
    Canada, determined for the annual period and in the manner that is
    prescribed, generated from all the assets that would be owned by the
    continuing entity that would result from the amalgamation or by
    entities controlled by the continuing entity would exceed the amount
    determined under subsection (7) or (8), as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General limit --- parties to
  amalgamation

  [(4.1)]{.lawlabel} This Part does not apply in respect of a proposed
  amalgamation of two or more entities if one or more of those entities
  carries on an operating business or controls an entity that carries on
  an operating business, unless each of at least two of the amalgamating
  entities, together with its affiliates,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} has assets in Canada, determined as of the time and
    in the manner that is prescribed, that exceed in aggregate value the
    amount determined under subsection (7) or (8), as the case may be;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} has gross revenues from sales in, from or into
    Canada, determined for the annual period and in the manner that is
    prescribed, that exceed in aggregate value the amount determined
    under subsection (7) or (8), as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Combination

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 112 and 113, this Part applies in
  respect of a proposed combination of two or more persons to carry on
  business otherwise than through a corporation if one or more of those
  persons, or one or more of their affiliates, proposes to contribute to
  the combination assets that form all or part of an operating business
  carried on by those persons or affiliates, and if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate value of the assets in Canada,
    determined as of the time and in the manner that is prescribed, that
    are the subject-matter of the combination would exceed the amount
    determined under subsection (7) or (8), as the case may be; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenues from sales in, from or into
    Canada, determined for the annual period and in the manner that is
    prescribed, generated from all the assets that are the subject
    matter of the combination would exceed the amount determined under
    subsection (7) or (8), as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Combination

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 111, 112 and 113, this Part
  applies in respect of a proposed acquisition of an interest in a
  combination that carries on an operating business otherwise than
  through a corporation

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the aggregate value of the assets in Canada,
      determined as of the time and in the manner that is prescribed,
      that are the subject-matter of the combination would exceed the
      amount determined under subsection (7) or (8), as the case may be,
      or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the gross revenues from sales in, from or into
      Canada, determined for the annual period and in the manner that is
      prescribed, generated from all the assets that are the subject
      matter of the combination would exceed the amount determined under
      subsection (7) or (8), as the case may be; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if, as a result of the proposed acquisition of the
    interest, the person or persons acquiring the interest, together
    with their affiliates, would hold an aggregate interest in the
    combination that entitles the person or persons to receive more than
    35% of the profits of the combination, or more than 35% of its
    assets on dissolution, or, if the person or persons acquiring the
    interest are already so entitled, to receive more than 50% of such
    profits or assets.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount for notification

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} In the year in which this subsection comes into
  force, the amount for the purposes of subsections (2) to (6) is
  \$70,000,000.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amount for notification --- subsequent
  years

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} In any year following the year in which
  subsection (7) comes into force, the amount for the purposes of any of
  subsections (2) to (6) is

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any amount that is prescribed for that subsection;
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if no amount has been prescribed for that
    subsection,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the amount determined by the Minister in January
      of that year by rounding off to the nearest million dollars the
      amount arrived at by using the formula

      ::: {#90128 .Subparagraph}
      A × (B / C)

      where

      [A]{.dfn}
      :   is the amount for the previous year,

      [B]{.dfn}
      :   is the average of the Nominal Gross Domestic Products at
          market prices for the most recent four consecutive quarters,
          and

      [C]{.dfn}
      :   is the average of the Nominal Gross Domestic Products at
          market prices for the four consecutive quarters for the
          comparable period in the year preceding the year used in
          calculating B, or
      :::

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} until the Minister has published under
      subsection (9) an amount for that year determined under
      subparagraph (i), if the Minister does so at all, the amount for
      that subsection for the previous year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication in [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} As soon as possible after determining the amount for
  any particular year, the Minister shall publish the amount in the
  [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/).

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 27
- 2009, c. 2, s. 436
- 2018, c. 8, s. 118
- [2024, c. 15, s. 261](#1462923-1483963){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462923-1483963.html"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-110-20180501.html "Link to previous version of section 110"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Exemptions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-90138 .Subheading}

#### [Acquisition of Voting Shares, Assets or Interests]{.HTitleText3} {#h-90139 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acquisitions

**[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111 .sectionLabel}** The following classes
of transactions are exempt from the application of this Part:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an acquisition of real property or goods in the
  ordinary course of business if the person or persons who propose to
  acquire the assets would not, as a result of the acquisition, hold all
  or substantially all of the assets of a business or of an operating
  segment of a business;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an acquisition of voting shares or of an interest in
  a combination solely for the purpose of underwriting the shares or the
  interest, within the meaning of subsection 5(2);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} an acquisition of voting shares, an interest in a
  combination or assets that would result from a gift, intestate
  succession or testamentary disposition;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} an acquisition of collateral or receivables, or an
  acquisition resulting from a foreclosure or default or forming part of
  a debt work-out, made by a creditor in or pursuant to a credit
  transaction entered into in good faith in the ordinary course of
  business;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} an acquisition of a Canadian resource property, as
  defined in subsection 66(15) of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3),
  pursuant to an agreement in writing that provides for the transfer of
  that property to the person or persons acquiring the property only if
  the person or persons acquiring the property incur expenses to carry
  out exploration or development activities with respect to the
  property; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} an acquisition of equity interests in an entity under
  an agreement in writing that provides for the creation of those equity
  interests only if the person or persons acquiring them incur expenses
  to carry out exploration or development activities with respect to a
  [[Canadian resource property]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in
  subsection 66(15) of the [Income Tax Act](/eng/acts/I-3.3), in respect
  of which the entity has the right to carry out those activities, if
  the entity does not have any significant assets other than that
  property.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1999, c. 2, s. 29, c. 31, s. 229
- 2018, c. 8, s. 119
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-111-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 111"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Combinations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-90150 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Combinations that are joint ventures

**[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** A combination is exempt
from the application of this Part if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} all the persons who propose to form the combination
  are parties to an agreement in writing or intended to be put in
  writing that imposes on one or more of them an obligation to
  contribute assets and governs a continuing relationship between those
  parties;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no change in control over any party to the
  combination would result from the combination; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the agreement referred to in paragraph (a) restricts
  the range of activities that may be carried on pursuant to the
  combination, and contains provisions that would allow for its orderly
  termination.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
:::

#### [General]{.HTitleText3} {#h-90158 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}General exemptions

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** The following classes
of transactions are exempt from the application of this Part:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a transaction all the parties to which are affiliates
  of each other;

- [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} a transaction in respect of which the Minister of
  Finance has certified to the Commissioner under paragraph 94(b) that
  it is, or would be, in the public interest;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a transaction in respect of which the Commissioner
  has issued a certificate under section 102;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} a transaction in respect of which the Commissioner or
  a person authorized by the Commissioner has waived, during the year
  preceding the day on which the transaction was completed, the
  obligation under this Part to notify the Commissioner and supply
  information because substantially similar information was previously
  supplied in relation to a request for a certificate under section 102;
  and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} such other classes of transactions as may be
  prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- R.S., 1985, c. 19 (2nd Supp.), s. 45
- 1991, c. 45, s. 550, c. 46, s. 594, c. 47, s. 717
- 1999, c. 2, ss. 30, 37
- 2001, c. 9, s. 580
- [2024, c. 15, s. 262](#1462938-1483971){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1462938-1483971.html"}
:::
